    ## The Rule
    
    **Invoke relevant or requested skills BEFORE any response or action** — including clarifying questions, exploring the codebase, or checking files. If it turns out wrong for the situation, you don't have to use it.
    
    **Before entering plan mode:** if you haven't already brainstormed, invoke the brainstorming skill first.
    
    Then announce "Using [skill] to [purpose]" and follow the skill exactly. If it has a checklist, create a todo per item.
    
    ## Skill Priority
    
    When multiple skills apply, process skills come first — they set the approach, then implementation skills (frontend-design, etc.) carry it out. Brainstorming and systematic-debugging are Superpowers' most common process skills, but the rule holds for any of them.
    
    - "Let's build X" → superpowers:brainstorming first, then implementation skills.
    - "Fix this bug" → superpowers:systematic-debugging first, then domain skills.
    
    ## Red Flags
    
    These thoughts mean STOP—you're rationalizing:
    
    | Thought | Reality |
    |---------|---------|
    | "This is just a simple question" | Questions are tasks. Check for skills. |
    | "I need more context first" | Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions. |
    | "Let me explore the codebase first" | Skills tell you HOW to explore. Check first. |
    | "I can check git/files quickly" | Files lack conversation context. Check for skills. |
    | "Let me gather information first" | Skills tell you HOW to gather information. |
    | "This doesn't need a formal skill" | If a skill exists, use it. |
    | "I remember this skill" | Skills evolve. Read current version. |
    | "This doesn't count as a task" | Action = task. Check for skills. |
    | "The skill is overkill" | Simple things become complex. Use it. |
    | "I'll just do this one thing first" | Check BEFORE doing anything. |
    | "This feels productive" | Undisciplined action wastes time. Skills prevent this. |
    | "I know what that means" | Knowing the concept ≠ using the skill. Invoke it. |

    ---
    
    # Brainstorming Ideas Into Designs
    
    Help turn ideas into fully formed designs and specs through natural collaborative dialogue.
    
    Start by understanding the current project context, then ask questions one at a time to refine the idea. Once you understand what you're building, present the design and get user approval.
    
    <HARD-GATE>
    Do NOT invoke any implementation skill, write any code, scaffold any project, or take any implementation action until you have presented a design and the user has approved it. This applies to EVERY project regardless of perceived simplicity.
    </HARD-GATE>
    
    ## Anti-Pattern: "This Is Too Simple To Need A Design"
    
    Every project goes through this process. A todo list, a single-function utility, a config change — all of them. "Simple" projects are where unexamined assumptions cause the most wasted work. The design can be short (a few sentences for truly simple projects), but you MUST present it and get approval.
    
    ## Checklist
    
    You MUST create a task for each of these items and complete them in order:
    
    1. **Explore project context** — check files, docs, recent commits
    2. **Offer visual companion** (if topic will involve visual questions) — this is its own message, not combined with a clarifying question. See the Visual Companion section below.
    3. **Ask clarifying questions** — one at a time, understand purpose/constraints/success criteria
    4. **Propose 2-3 approaches** — with trade-offs and your recommendation
    5. **Present design** — in sections scaled to their complexity, get user approval after each section
    6. **Write design doc** — save to `docs/superpowers/specs/YYYY-MM-DD-<topic>-design.md` and commit
    7. **Spec self-review** — quick inline check for placeholders, contradictions, ambiguity, scope (see below)
    8. **User reviews written spec** — ask user to review the spec file before proceeding
    9. **Transition to implementation** — invoke writing-plans skill to create implementation plan
    
    ## Process Flow
    
    ```dot
    digraph brainstorming {
        "Explore project context" [shape=box];
        "Visual questions ahead?" [shape=diamond];
        "Offer Visual Companion\n(own message, no other content)" [shape=box];
        "Ask clarifying questions" [shape=box];
        "Propose 2-3 approaches" [shape=box];
        "Present design sections" [shape=box];
        "User approves design?" [shape=diamond];
        "Write design doc" [shape=box];
        "Spec self-review\n(fix inline)" [shape=box];
        "User reviews spec?" [shape=diamond];
        "Invoke writing-plans skill" [shape=doublecircle];
    
        "Explore project context" -> "Visual questions ahead?";
        "Visual questions ahead?" -> "Offer Visual Companion\n(own message, no other content)" [label="yes"];
        "Visual questions ahead?" -> "Ask clarifying questions" [label="no"];
        "Offer Visual Companion\n(own message, no other content)" -> "Ask clarifying questions";
        "Ask clarifying questions" -> "Propose 2-3 approaches";
        "Propose 2-3 approaches" -> "Present design sections";
        "Present design sections" -> "User approves design?";
        "User approves design?" -> "Present design sections" [label="no, revise"];
        "User approves design?" -> "Write design doc" [label="yes"];
        "Write design doc" -> "Spec self-review\n(fix inline)";
        "Spec self-review\n(fix inline)" -> "User reviews spec?";
        "User reviews spec?" -> "Write design doc" [label="changes requested"];
        "User reviews spec?" -> "Invoke writing-plans skill" [label="approved"];
    }
    ```
    
    **The terminal state is invoking writing-plans.** Do NOT invoke frontend-design, mcp-builder, or any other implementation skill. The ONLY skill you invoke after brainstorming is writing-plans.
    
    ## The Process
    
    **Understanding the idea:**
    
    - Check out the current project state first (files, docs, recent commits)
    - Before asking detailed questions, assess scope: if the request describes multiple independent subsystems (e.g., "build a platform with chat, file storage, billing, and analytics"), flag this immediately. Don't spend questions refining details of a project that needs to be decomposed first.
    - If the project is too large for a single spec, help the user decompose into sub-projects: what are the independent pieces, how do they relate, what order should they be built? Then brainstorm the first sub-project through the normal design flow. Each sub-project gets its own spec → plan → implementation cycle.
    - For appropriately-scoped projects, ask questions one at a time to refine the idea
    - Prefer multiple choice questions when possible, but open-ended is fine too
    - Only one question per message - if a topic needs more exploration, break it into multiple questions
    - Focus on understanding: purpose, constraints, success criteria
    
    **Exploring approaches:**
    
    - Propose 2-3 different approaches with trade-offs
    - Present options conversationally with your recommendation and reasoning
    - Lead with your recommended option and explain why
    
    **Presenting the design:**
    
    - Once you believe you understand what you're building, present the design
    - Scale each section to its complexity: a few sentences if straightforward, up to 200-300 words if nuanced
    - Ask after each section whether it looks right so far
    - Cover: architecture, components, data flow, error handling, testing
    - Be ready to go back and clarify if something doesn't make sense
    
    **Design for isolation and clarity:**
    
    - Break the system into smaller units that each have one clear purpose, communicate through well-defined interfaces, and can be understood and tested independently
    - For each unit, you should be able to answer: what does it do, how do you use it, and what does it depend on?
    - Can someone understand what a unit does without reading its internals? Can you change the internals without breaking consumers? If not, the boundaries need work.
    - Smaller, well-bounded units are also easier for you to work with - you reason better about code you can hold in context at once, and your edits are more reliable when files are focused. When a file grows large, that's often a signal that it's doing too much.
    
    **Working in existing codebases:**
    
    - Explore the current structure before proposing changes. Follow existing patterns.
    - Where existing code has problems that affect the work (e.g., a file that's grown too large, unclear boundaries, tangled responsibilities), include targeted improvements as part of the design - the way a good developer improves code they're working in.
    - Don't propose unrelated refactoring. Stay focused on what serves the current goal.
    
    ## After the Design
    
    **Documentation:**
    
    - Write the validated design (spec) to `docs/superpowers/specs/YYYY-MM-DD-<topic>-design.md`
      - (User preferences for spec location override this default)
    - Use elements-of-style:writing-clearly-and-concisely skill if available
    - Commit the design document to git
    
    **Spec Self-Review:**
    After writing the spec document, look at it with fresh eyes:
    
    1. **Placeholder scan:** Any "TBD", "TODO", incomplete sections, or vague requirements? Fix them.
    2. **Internal consistency:** Do any sections contradict each other? Does the architecture match the feature descriptions?
    3. **Scope check:** Is this focused enough for a single implementation plan, or does it need decomposition?
    4. **Ambiguity check:** Could any requirement be interpreted two different ways? If so, pick one and make it explicit.
    
    Fix any issues inline. No need to re-review — just fix and move on.
    
    **User Review Gate:**
    After the spec review loop passes, ask the user to review the written spec before proceeding:
    
    > "Spec written and committed to `<path>`. Please review it and let me know if you want to make any changes before we start writing out the implementation plan."
    
    Wait for the user's response. If they request changes, make them and re-run the spec review loop. Only proceed once the user approves.
    
    **Implementation:**
    
    - Invoke the writing-plans skill to create a detailed implementation plan
    - Do NOT invoke any other skill. writing-plans is the next step.
    
    ## Key Principles
    
    - **One question at a time** - Don't overwhelm with multiple questions
    - **Multiple choice preferred** - Easier to answer than open-ended when possible
    - **YAGNI ruthlessly** - Remove unnecessary features from all designs
    - **Explore alternatives** - Always propose 2-3 approaches before settling
    - **Incremental validation** - Present design, get approval before moving on
    - **Be flexible** - Go back and clarify when something doesn't make sense
    
    ## Visual Companion
    
    A browser-based companion for showing mockups, diagrams, and visual options during brainstorming. Available as a tool — not a mode. Accepting the companion means it's available for questions that benefit from visual treatment; it does NOT mean every question goes through the browser.
    
    **Offering the companion:** When you anticipate that upcoming questions will involve visual content (mockups, layouts, diagrams), offer it once for consent:
    > "Some of what we're working on might be easier to explain if I can show it to you in a web browser. I can put together mockups, diagrams, comparisons, and other visuals as we go. This feature is still new and can be token-intensive. Want to try it? (Requires opening a local URL)"
    
    **This offer MUST be its own message.** Do not combine it with clarifying questions, context summaries, or any other content. The message should contain ONLY the offer above and nothing else. Wait for the user's response before continuing. If they decline, proceed with text-only brainstorming.
    
    **Per-question decision:** Even after the user accepts, decide FOR EACH QUESTION whether to use the browser or the terminal. The test: **would the user understand this better by seeing it than reading it?**
    
    - **Use the browser** for content that IS visual — mockups, wireframes, layout comparisons, architecture diagrams, side-by-side visual designs
    - **Use the terminal** for content that is text — requirements questions, conceptual choices, tradeoff lists, A/B/C/D text options, scope decisions
    
    A question about a UI topic is not automatically a visual question. "What does personality mean in this context?" is a conceptual question — use the terminal. "Which wizard layout works better?" is a visual question — use the browser.
    
    If they agree to the companion, read the detailed guide before proceeding:
    `skills/brainstorming/visual-companion.md`

    ## Subagent dispatch requires multi-agent support
    
    Add to your Codex config (`~/.codex/config.toml`):
    
    ```toml
    [features]
    multi_agent = true
    ```
    
    This enables the multi-agent tools that skills like
    `dispatching-parallel-agents` and `subagent-driven-development` use.
    Which tools you get depends on the multi-agent version your model
    preset selects (current presets run V2; older ones run V1). Trust your
    actual tool list over any table — including this one — when they
    disagree.
    
    - **Spawning:** give children a clean context with
      `spawn_agent {fork_turns: "none"}`; the default `"all"` copies your
      entire transcript into the child. On Codex 0.145+, role files under
      `~/.codex/agents/` attach to isolated forks via `agent_type`.
      Full-history forks accept `model` and `reasoning_effort` overrides
      (only `agent_type` is refused there) — isolated forks are the SDD
      default for context hygiene, not because overrides require them.
    - **Fix rounds:** resume the implementer with `followup_task` — it
      delivers your message, triggers a turn, and transparently reloads a
      child the harness evicted. Never dispatch a fresh implementer on the
      theory that a spawned agent cannot be messaged again; on V2 it
      always can.
    - **Lifecycle:** V2 has no `close_agent`. Finished children are
      evicted automatically when slots are needed; leaving them unclosed
      costs nothing. Only V1 sessions have `close_agent` — there, close
      reviewers when their review returns, and close each implementer
      after its task's review passes.
    - **Model names:** never copy a model name from a skill, table, or old
      session into `spawn_agent` without checking it against your current
      spawn allowlist — V2 accepts only V2-capable presets and hard-errors
      on the rest.
    
    ## Waiting on children
    
    `wait_agent` is an event subscription, not a poll: a long wait wakes
    the moment a child produces mailbox activity, with the same latency as
    a short one. Short-timeout polling buys nothing and costs a tool call —
    and a context rebill — per poll. In measured sessions, roughly
    two-thirds of all wait calls were short polls that timed out.
    
    - While you still have local work, do not wait at all. A completed
      child's final answer is pushed into your mailbox and arrives with
      your next turn.
    - When you are genuinely idle with children outstanding, wait in
      bounded stretches: `wait_agent` with `timeout_ms` 300000-600000
      (5-10 minutes). After each stretch — wake or timeout — post one
      status line, run `list_agents`, and chase any child that finished
      without reporting. Never stack polls shorter than five minutes; the
      event subscription wakes a bounded stretch just as fast as a short
      one.
    - Completion mail cannot wake an idle controller (it is delivered
      without triggering a turn); covering that idle window is
      `wait_agent`'s only job. A stretch that times out with no activity
      is your cue to reconcile, not to shorten the next stretch.
    
    ## Model routing on spawns
    
    Every `spawn_agent` you issue — including when you are yourself a
    spawned child running a fan-out — sets `model` AND `reasoning_effort`
    explicitly, per the Model Selection rules of the skill you are
    executing. Setting `model` alone is a trap: the child's effort
    silently resets to that model's default, not to yours.
    
    Ask your human partner to add a machine-level backstop to
    `~/.codex/config.toml` so any spawn that slips through still routes to
    a deliberate tier instead of silently inheriting the session's most
    expensive model:
    
    ```toml
    [agents]
    default_subagent_model = "<a mid-tier model from your spawn allowlist>"
    default_subagent_reasoning_effort = "medium"
    ```
    
    ## Environment Detection
    
    Skills that create worktrees or finish branches should detect their
    environment with read-only git commands before proceeding:
    
    ```bash
    GIT_DIR=$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-dir)" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)
    GIT_COMMON=$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)
    BRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)
    ```
    
    - `GIT_DIR != GIT_COMMON` → already in a linked worktree (skip creation)
    - `BRANCH` empty → detached HEAD (cannot branch/push/PR from sandbox)
    
    See `using-git-worktrees` Step 0 and `finishing-a-development-branch`
    Step 1 for how each skill uses these signals.
    
    ## Codex App Finishing
    
    When the sandbox blocks branch/push operations (detached HEAD in an
    externally managed worktree), the agent commits all work and informs
    the user to use the App's native controls:
    
    - **"Create branch"** — names the branch, then commit/push/PR via App UI
    - **"Hand off to local"** — transfers work to the user's local checkout
    
    The agent can still run tests, stage files, and output suggested branch
    names, commit messages, and PR descriptions for the user to copy.

    # AI Vibe Coding V3.0
    
    ## WeBox — 企业员工餐食订购平台（全栈合并版 PRD）
    
    ### 产品需求文档 (PRD)
    
    | 项目     | 内容                     |
    | -------- | ------------------------ |
    | 文档版本 | V3.0（前后端合并）        |
    | 创建日期 | 2026-03-24               |
    | 场景     | 1.5 小时 AI 全栈开发挑战 |
    | 适用岗位 | 前端 / 后端（同一份 PRD，均要求完整前后端交付；评分侧重按岗位区分，详见附录 A） |
    
    ---
    
    ## 1. 产品背景
    
    WeBox 是一个企业内部的员工餐食订购平台。公司约 200 名员工，食堂每日提供菜品供员工选择，菜品库会持续扩充，当前约 50 个菜品。**员工通过手机和电脑两种设备访问平台**，提前选餐下单，食堂据此备餐。
    
    **每天上午 9:30-10:00 是订餐高峰期**，大部分员工会在这个时段集中下单午餐，菜单数据在此期间被高频访问。
    
    平台有两类用户，**使用同一套系统，但拥有不同的功能权限**：
    - **员工**：浏览菜单、搜索菜品、下单订餐
    - **管理员**：通过 **Console 后台管理模块**管理菜品、处理订单、了解平台经营情况
    
    > **系统语言要求（强制）**：本系统面向用户的**所有内容一律使用英文**，包括但不限于——界面文案与按钮、导航与页面标题、菜单数据（菜品名称/描述/分类/过敏原/辣度/选项）、表单标签与占位符、提示与错误信息（toast / 弹窗 / 校验提示）、AI 推荐理由、数据看板文案等。本 PRD 中出现的中文示例字符串仅用于说明语义，实现时须以对应英文呈现。
    
    ---
    
    ## 2. 需求分层
    
    本项目需求分为三层，候选人按顺序完成。**核心功能为必须完成项，进阶功能为主要考察项，挑战功能为可选加分项**——请优先保证核心与进阶功能完整可用，行有余力再挑战 Tier 3。**注意：无论选择完成到哪一层，都必须交付完整可运行的前后端（全栈交付为强制要求）。**
    
    | 层级       | 范围                                       | 是否必做 |
    | ---------- | ------------------------------------------ | -------- |
    | 核心功能（Tier 1） | 员工端：注册登录、菜单浏览与搜索、购物车、下单 | ✅ 必做 |
    | 进阶功能（Tier 2） | 员工端：偏好设置 + 订餐规则；Console 后台：菜品管理  | ✅ 主要考察项 |
    | 挑战功能（Tier 3） | 库存实时展示与扣减、AI 智能推荐、经营数据看板  | ⭐ 可选加分项 |
    
    ---
    
    ## 3. 核心功能
    
    ### 3.1 员工注册 / 登录
    
    员工使用企业邮箱注册账号（需通过邮箱格式校验；**不强制校验特定公司域名**，同一邮箱不可重复注册）。注册后可登录系统。刷新页面不应丢失登录状态。注册、登录过程中的异常情况（如邮箱已被注册、密码不正确等）需要给予用户明确的反馈。
    
    密码需满足基本安全要求（**至少 8 位，且同时包含字母与数字**），且**不得以明文形式存储**。注册和登录表单需对用户输入做校验与长度限制（邮箱格式校验；各文本输入设合理长度上限——如邮箱/地址 ≤ 200 字符、搜索关键词 ≤ 50 字符），并对特殊字符做安全处理。系统需要区分员工和管理员两种角色，不同角色可访问的功能不同。
    
    ### 3.2 每日菜单
    
    员工进入系统后看到的主页面是当日菜单。菜单以卡片形式展示菜品，每个卡片包含菜品图片、名称、价格（精确到分，如 ¥22.50）和菜系标签。
    
    点击卡片可查看菜品详情，详情页包含：
    - 菜品描述
    - 成分信息：蛋白质来源（如鸡肉、牛肉、鱼肉、豆制品等）、过敏原、辣度等级（不辣/微辣/中辣/重辣）
    - 自定义选项（如有）：部分菜品支持加料或配置选择。例如汉堡可选择酱料（番茄酱/芥末酱/蛋黄酱/BBQ酱）和面胚（普通/全麦），意面可选择面型和加料（培根 +¥5/芝士 +¥3）。每个选项组可能是必选（如面胚）或可选（如加料），部分选项有额外费用。员工选择后，菜品价格需实时更新。
    
    员工可以按菜系筛选菜品（分类以英文展示：`Chinese`、`Western`、`Japanese`、`Light Meal`、`Korean`、`Southeast Asian` 等）。筛选时支持多选，例如同时查看 `Chinese` 和 `Japanese`。
    
    员工可以通过搜索框搜索菜品，输入菜品名称或描述中的关键词查找菜品。
    
    **注意**：菜品数量会持续增长，菜单数据在高峰期被高频访问，页面需要在菜品数量较多、访问量较高时仍能流畅使用。
    
    ### 3.3 购物车
    
    员工在菜单页可以将菜品加入购物车。同一菜品可重复添加，每次点击数量加一。购物车内可以调整每个菜品的数量，也可以移除菜品。购物车需实时显示当前总价。
    
    如果菜品有自定义选项，购物车中需展示员工已选择的配置（如"全麦面胚 + 芥末酱"）。同一菜品不同配置视为不同条目（例如一个全麦汉堡和一个普通汉堡分别显示）。
    
    **注意**：早高峰时段（9:30-10:00）是订餐高峰，大量员工会频繁点击"加入购物车"按钮。
    
    ### 3.4 下单
    
    员工从购物车进入结算页面。结算页展示订单明细（菜品、自定义选项、数量、小计含加料费用、总价），员工需要选择配送日期（默认当天）、用餐时段（`Lunch` 或 `Dinner`）、配送地址（可从历史地址选择或输入新地址）。
    
    确认无误后提交订单。**订单提交必须保证每次请求只会创建一个订单**，即使员工因网络延迟连续点击了多次"提交"按钮。提交成功后展示订单成功页面，包含订单编号和预计配送信息（配送日期、用餐时段、配送地址）。
    
    员工可以在"我的订单"页面查看所有历史订单，点击可查看订单详情（含每个菜品的所选自定义选项、数量、小计与总价）。订单支持取消操作（仅限状态为 `Pending` 的订单）。订单状态英文枚举：`Pending`、`Confirmed`、`Completed`、`Cancelled`。
    
    ---
    
    ## 4. 进阶功能
    
    ### 4.1 饮食偏好设置
    
    员工可以在个人设置中配置饮食偏好：
    
    - **过敏原标记**：标记自己的过敏原（枚举与菜品过敏原字段一致：`Peanuts`、`Dairy`、`Egg`、`Gluten`、`Soy`、`Fish`、`Shellfish`）。当员工将含有已标记过敏原的菜品加入购物车时，系统需弹窗提醒（英文，例如 `This dish contains an allergen you flagged: [Egg]. Add anyway?`），由员工自行决定是否继续。注意：**不是过滤掉含过敏原的菜品**，而是在加入时提醒。
    - **菜系偏好**：选择偏好的菜系；当开启下文的「为我推荐」开关时，菜单页优先展示偏好菜系的菜品（关闭时按默认顺序展示）。
    - **口味偏好**：辣度偏好（不辣/微辣/中辣/重辣，与菜品辣度枚举 `None/Mild/Medium/Hot` 一一对应，用于菜单排序/高亮）、口味浓淡偏好（清淡/适中/重口，主要作为 AI 推荐 §5.2 的个性化参考，不强制驱动菜单硬排序）。
    - **单餐预算**：设置每餐的预算范围，如 ¥15-¥30。当购物车总价超出预算上限时，在结算页给予提示（但不阻止下单）。
    
    菜单页提供"为我推荐"开关，开启后根据偏好自动排序和高亮匹配菜品。
    
    ### 4.2 订餐规则
    
    食堂运营有以下规则，系统需要严格执行：
    
    - **每单数量上限**：每个订单最多包含 5 份餐品（注意是总份数，不是品种数。比如 3 份宫保鸡丁 + 3 份麻婆豆腐 = 6 份，超限）。达到上限后，菜单页的"加入购物车"按钮和购物车内的"+"按钮都应该不可用，并说明原因。
    - **截单时间**：午餐订单须在当日上午 10:00 前提交，晚餐订单须在当日下午 15:00 前提交。过了某餐次的截单时间，系统应**自动切换到时间上最近的可订餐次**，判定规则为：**当日晚餐若未过 15:00 → 切换为当日晚餐；当日晚餐也已截单 → 切换为次日午餐**。例如上午 10:30 提交午餐订单（午餐已截单、晚餐未截单）→ 自动切换为当日晚餐。
    - **不可重复下单**：同一天同一餐次（如周三午餐），每位员工只能有一个有效订单（`Pending` 或 `Confirmed` 状态）。如果已有有效订单，结算页的 `Place Order` 按钮应变为 `View Existing Order` 并跳转。
    
    ### 4.3 Console 后台管理模块 — 菜品管理
    
    管理员登录后进入 **Console 后台管理模块**（英文 UI 名 `Console`，与员工端使用不同的导航布局）。**普通员工不可访问 Console 后台功能。**
    
    **菜品管理页面**：
    - 以表格形式展示所有菜品，支持搜索和按分类筛选
    - 可新增菜品：填写名称、描述、价格、分类、过敏原标签，上传图片
    - 可编辑已有菜品信息
    - 可设置菜品的"上架/下架"状态，下架的菜品员工端不可见
    - 可设置每日菜单：选择哪些菜品出现在指定日期（默认次日）的菜单中，并为每个菜品设置该日供应数量
    
    > **数据初始化说明**：系统须预置**当日可下单的菜单与库存种子数据**，供演示当天直接完成下单、库存扣减、看板统计；「设置每日菜单」既可面向次日、也可用于初始化或调整当日菜单。评分演示以「当日可下单菜单」为准。
    
    ---
    
    ## 5. 挑战功能（可选加分项）
    
    > 本层为**可选加分项，不计入功能完成度门槛**。未完成不影响基础评级，完成则作为加分与技术亮点。建议在核心 + 进阶功能完整可用后再投入。
    
    ### 5.1 库存、实时展示与扣减
    
    每个菜品每日有限量供应。菜单页需展示每个菜品的剩余数量。当剩余数量较少时（≤ 3 份），需要有视觉提示。售罄的菜品不可下单。
    
    **关键要求**：
    - **多个员工可能同时下单同一个菜品，库存扣减不能出现超卖。**
    - 当一位员工下单后，其他正在浏览菜单的员工应当能够**实时看到库存变化，而不需要手动刷新页面**。
    - 提交订单时需要再次确认库存充足，如果提交时发现库存不足，需要告知员工具体哪些菜品库存不足，并允许调整后重新提交。
    - 订单取消后，对应库存应恢复。
    
    ### 5.2 AI 智能推荐
    
    在菜单页面提供一个 AI 选餐助手入口。员工可以用自然语言（英文）描述想吃什么（例如 `I want something light today` / `A high-protein low-fat lunch` / `Nothing I ate yesterday`），AI 助手根据以下信息给出个性化推荐：
    
    - 员工的饮食偏好设置（如已配置）
    - 当日可用的菜品（排除已售罄的）
    - 员工的过敏原（含过敏原的菜品不应出现在推荐中）
    - 员工近期（**近 7 天**）订餐记录（避免重复推荐近期已点过的菜品）
    
    推荐结果以菜品卡片形式展示（不是纯文字），每个推荐附带个性化的推荐理由，员工可直接将推荐菜品加入购物车。推荐过程应有流式的响应体验，而非长时间等待后一次性展示。
    
    **注意**：本功能需要实际接入 LLM 服务，候选人需自行准备 LLM API Key 或本地模型，面试现场不提供。该功能必须实际可运行。
    
    ### 5.3 Console 后台管理模块 — 经营数据看板
    
    Console 后台管理模块增加一个数据看板页面，展示食堂的经营状况：
    
    - **今日概览**：今日订单数、总收入、已完成/待处理/已取消订单数
    - **菜品排行**：按销量排序的菜品 Top 10，展示为柱状图
    - **时段分布**：午餐 vs 晚餐的订单量对比
    - **趋势图**：最近 7 天的订单量和收入趋势折线图
    - **库存预警**：当前库存 ≤ 3 份的菜品列表（低库存阈值与 §5.1 一致：售罄 = 0，低库存 = 1~3）
    
    看板数据应能实时刷新（或提供手动刷新按钮），且数据须来自真实订单。
    
    ---
    
    ## 6. 菜品数据
    
    候选人可使用以下菜品信息或自行补充。**入库与展示均须使用英文**（下表即为系统中应存储/展示的英文内容）：
    
    | Name | Description | Price | Category | Protein | Allergens | Spice Level | Customization Options |
    |------|-------------|-------|----------|---------|-----------|-------------|-----------------------|
    | Kung Pao Chicken | Classic Sichuan dish, chicken stir-fried with peanuts and dried chili | ¥22.00 | Chinese | Chicken | Peanuts | Medium | — |
    | Caesar Salad | Fresh romaine lettuce with Parmesan and Caesar dressing | ¥28.50 | Light Meal | None | Dairy, Egg | None | Add-ons: Grilled Chicken (+¥6) / Bacon (+¥5) / Avocado (+¥4) |
    | Salmon Sashimi Set | Fresh salmon sashimi with rice and miso soup | ¥45.00 | Japanese | Fish | Fish | None | — |
    | Tomato Pasta | Classic Italian tomato pasta with fresh basil | ¥26.50 | Western | None | Gluten | None | Pasta Type (required): Spaghetti / Fusilli / Penne; Add-ons: Bacon (+¥5) / Cheese (+¥3) |
    | Tom Yum Soup | Thai hot & sour shrimp soup with lemongrass, galangal, lime leaves | ¥32.00 | Southeast Asian | Shrimp | Shellfish | Hot | — |
    | Chicken Quinoa Bowl | Low-fat high-protein grilled chicken with quinoa, avocado, veggies | ¥35.80 | Light Meal | Chicken | None | None | Base (required): Quinoa / Brown Rice / Mixed Grains |
    | Mapo Tofu | Sichuan classic, soft tofu with spicy minced meat | ¥18.00 | Chinese | Tofu, Pork | Soy | Medium | — |
    | Korean Bibimbap | Stone-pot mixed rice with veggies, fried egg, and chili sauce | ¥30.00 | Korean | Egg | Egg, Soy | Mild | Add-ons: Cheese (+¥3) / Fried Egg (+¥2) / Beef Slices (+¥8) |
    | Classic Beef Burger | Angus beef patty with lettuce, tomato, onion | ¥38.00 | Western | Beef | Gluten, Dairy | None | Bun (required): Plain / Whole Wheat; Sauce (required): Ketchup / Mustard / Mayo / BBQ; Add-ons: Cheese (+¥3) / Bacon (+¥5) / Fried Egg (+¥2) |
    
    > 分类枚举（英文）：`Chinese`、`Western`、`Japanese`、`Light Meal`、`Korean`、`Southeast Asian`。
    > 辣度枚举（英文）：`None`、`Mild`、`Medium`、`Hot`。
    > 过敏原枚举（英文，固定全集）：`Peanuts`、`Dairy`、`Egg`、`Gluten`、`Soy`、`Fish`、`Shellfish`（海鲜类统一用 `Fish` / `Shellfish`，不单列 `Seafood`，以免偏好标记与菜品标签不匹配）。
    > 货币：价格统一使用人民币、精确到分，货币符号保留 `¥`（如 `¥22.50`）；金额展示与计算须避免浮点精度问题。
    
    菜品图片资源见附件 `webox_product_images.zip`。
    
    ---
    
    ## 7. 交付标准
    
    本项目定位为企业级应用，不是演示 Demo。技术选型和实现质量需要满足生产环境运行标准。
    
    ### 7.1 技术选型要求
    
    - **本项目要求完整实现前后端**，不能只做前端或只做后端，必须是可完整运行的前后端项目
    - 前端技术栈不限，需说明选型理由
    - 后端使用 **Java（JDK 17）+ Spring Boot** 框架
    - 数据持久化使用 **独立部署的 MySQL 服务**，**不接受嵌入式 / 内存数据库**（如 H2、SQLite、HSQLDB、Derby、嵌入式 MySQL 等一律不可）；MySQL 须作为独立进程真实运行，并可按 README 步骤连接
    - **缓存方案不限，Redis 为可选项**：菜单等高频数据缓存、会话/令牌、库存与幂等控制等，可选用 Redis，也可用进程内缓存或其他方案，**不强制使用 Redis**。若引入 Redis，需在 README 给出启动步骤
    - AI 推荐功能需要实际接入 LLM 服务（候选人自行准备，面试现场不提供 API Key），该功能必须可实际运行
    - **系统语言**：所有面向用户的内容（界面文案、菜单数据、错误/提示信息、AI 推荐理由等）**一律使用英文**（参见 §1 系统语言要求）
    - 可使用任意 AI 编码工具辅助开发
    
    **目标架构**：`前端 SPA → 后端服务（JDK 17 + Spring Boot）→ 独立 MySQL 服务（持久化）` 为**强制**基础设施；缓存层（如 Redis）为**可选增强**，用与不用不影响功能门槛。README 须给出 JDK + Spring Boot 应用 + MySQL 的完整本地启动步骤（若引入 Redis 等额外服务，一并说明）。
    
    ### 7.2 交付物清单
    
    候选人需要交付以下完整内容：
    
    | 交付物 | 要求 |
    |--------|------|
    | 可运行代码 | 项目可在本地完整启动和运行 |
    | README | 项目介绍、技术栈说明、本地启动步骤（确保他人能从零开始启动项目） |
    | API 文档 | 接口清单、请求/响应格式说明（格式不限） |
    | 架构说明 | 系统架构图或文字说明，包含核心模块划分和数据流 |
    | 测试 | 核心业务逻辑的测试用例，可运行并通过 |
    | **AI 编程对话记录** | **从项目开始到结束的完整原始对话导出**（Cursor / Claude Code / ChatGPT / Copilot Chat / Windsurf / Cline 等任意工具的原生导出文件均可）。**不接受截图、节选、片段或事后整理摘要**。多个工具混用时全部提交。存放于项目根目录 `ai-conversations/` 子目录，或在 README 中明确指出存放路径。**该项缺失或不达标将直接影响最终评级，请务必妥善保存。** |
    
    ### 7.3 项目定位
    
    **这是一个长期运行的项目，你是这个项目的 Owner。** 请从项目长期稳定运行的角度考虑你的设计和实现，确保项目具备可维护性和可靠性。
    
    ### 7.4 演示要求
    
    1.5 小时后，候选人需要：
    - 演示已完成的功能
    - 展示交付文档（含 AI 编程对话记录）
    - 简要说明技术选型理由
    - 说明遇到的问题和解决方式
    
    ---
    
    ## 附录 A：岗位评分侧重（面试官内部使用，不对候选人公开）
    
    > 本 PRD 为前端 / 后端候选人共用同一份，业务内容一致，**均要求完整前后端交付**（JDK 17 + Spring Boot + 独立 MySQL 架构，Redis 等缓存层为可选增强；全栈交付为强制要求，不因岗位或完成层级豁免）。产品优化（10）维度前后端评分标准相同；**主要差异在「技术方案 P6+（20 分）」的考察侧重，以及后端架构相关评分对前端岗位的放宽**——面试官按候选人**实际报考岗位**选择对应的 P6+ 考察表与架构评分口径。AI 对话记录门槛、下单级联归零等铁律对两类岗位一视同仁（详见评分指南 §1.3）。
    
    ### A.0 功能完成度口径（Tier 3 加分化后）
    
    > 挑战功能（Tier 3）已调整为**可选加分项**，不再计入功能完成度基数与门槛。功能完成度重新以 **Tier 1 + Tier 2 = 50 分** 为基数：
    
    | 项目 | 分值 | 说明 |
    |------|------|------|
    | Tier 1 核心功能 | 30 | 必做，端到端可用才计分 |
    | Tier 2 进阶功能 | 20 | 主要考察项，端到端可用才计分 |
    | **功能完成度基数** | **50** | 门槛与评级按此基数计算 |
    | Tier 3 挑战功能 | **+10（加分）** | 可选，做了加分、不做不扣；加分后功能维度上限仍以 60 封顶 |
    
    **功能完成度门槛（90%）随基数调整为 ≥ 45 / 50**：
    
    | 功能完成度（基数 50） | 最高可达评级 |
    |----------------------|------------|
    | ≥ 45 分（90%） | 按总分正常评级 |
    | 40–44 分（80–89%） | 最高「不推荐」，无论总分多高 |
    | < 40 分（< 80%） | 直接「不通过」 |
    
    > 说明一：Tier 3 中依赖下单的项（实时同步、库存扣减、看板真实数据、并发安全等）**级联归零规则依旧适用**——只是这些项现在归在「加分」而非「门槛」，下单不通仅使对应加分/ P6+ 项归零，不再直接拉低功能门槛基数。
    >
    > 说明二：P6+（20 分）中的「并发安全」「下单幂等」等仍照常考察，其业务锚点（同时下单不超卖、幂等提交）在 Tier 1「下单」与 Tier 3「库存」中均有体现；即使候选人未做 Tier 3 的实时/看板，只要下单与库存扣减可用，仍可验证这些 P6+ 项。
    
    ### A.1 前端候选人 · P6+ 考察侧重（20 分）
    
    | 考察点 | 分值 | PRD 中的业务锚点 |
    |--------|------|-----------------|

    | **功能完成度基数** | **50** | 门槛与评级按此基数计算 |
    | Tier 3 挑战功能 | **+10（加分）** | 可选，做了加分、不做不扣；加分后功能维度上限仍以 60 封顶 |
    
    **功能完成度门槛（90%）随基数调整为 ≥ 45 / 50**：
    
    | 功能完成度（基数 50） | 最高可达评级 |
    |----------------------|------------|
    | ≥ 45 分（90%） | 按总分正常评级 |
    | 40–44 分（80–89%） | 最高「不推荐」，无论总分多高 |
    | < 40 分（< 80%） | 直接「不通过」 |
    
    > 说明一：Tier 3 中依赖下单的项（实时同步、库存扣减、看板真实数据、并发安全等）**级联归零规则依旧适用**——只是这些项现在归在「加分」而非「门槛」，下单不通仅使对应加分/ P6+ 项归零，不再直接拉低功能门槛基数。
    >
    > 说明二：P6+（20 分）中的「并发安全」「下单幂等」等仍照常考察，其业务锚点（同时下单不超卖、幂等提交）在 Tier 1「下单」与 Tier 3「库存」中均有体现；即使候选人未做 Tier 3 的实时/看板，只要下单与库存扣减可用，仍可验证这些 P6+ 项。
    
    ### A.1 前端候选人 · P6+ 考察侧重（20 分）
    
    | 考察点 | 分值 | PRD 中的业务锚点 |
    |--------|------|-----------------|
    | 多屏幕适配 / 响应式 | 4 | §1「员工通过手机和电脑两种设备访问」 |
    | 分页 / 列表性能 | 4 | §3.2「菜品约 50 个且持续增长，数量较多时仍能流畅使用」 |
    | 按钮防抖 / 节流 | 4 | §3.3「早高峰频繁点击加入购物车」 |
    | 价格精度处理 | 4 | §3.2「价格精确到分，如 ¥22.50」 |
    | 输入校验与限制 | 4 | §3.1 注册/地址、§3.2 搜索框 |
    
    ### A.2 后端候选人 · P6+ 考察侧重（20 分）
    
    | 考察点 | 分值 | PRD 中的业务锚点 |
    |--------|------|-----------------|
    | 缓存策略 | 4 | §1「9:30-10:00 高峰集中下单」+ §3.2「菜单高频访问」；对菜单等热点数据做缓存并有合理的更新/失效策略（Redis 或进程内缓存等方案均可，不限定 Redis） |
    | 下单幂等 | 4 | §3.4「每次请求只会创建一个订单，即使连续点击多次提交」 |
    | SQL 注入防护 | 4 | §3.2「员工可搜索菜品」 |
    | 用户权限校验 | 4 | §1 / §3.1 / §4.3「两类用户不同权限，员工不可访问 Console 后台」 |
    | 并发安全（防超卖） | 4 | §5.1「多个员工同时下单同一菜品，不能超卖」 |
    
    > 说明：合并版 PRD 已同时包含前后端两套业务锚点（高峰并发 + 多端访问、幂等提交 + 价格精度等），因此**同一份文档发给任一岗位都能完整承载其 P6+ 考察点**，无需再区分发放。评分时仅需按岗位切换附录 A.1 / A.2 对应的 P6+ 表格即可。
    
    ### A.3 前端候选人 · 后端架构评分放宽
    
    > 前端岗位的核心考察在前端工程能力（A.1），后端架构不作为其能力评判重点。因此对前端候选人，**后端架构相关项按「能合理接入并端到端跑通即可」的标准评分，不做深度/最优性考察**：
    
    | 架构相关项 | 后端候选人口径 | **前端候选人（放宽）口径** |
    |-----------|--------------|--------------------------|
    | 缓存方案（含可选 Redis） | 若做缓存，考察更新与失效策略、命中率、一致性；Redis 为可选方案之一 | **是否做缓存/是否用 Redis 均不作为前端评分点，不考察策略优劣** |
    | 缓存策略（P6+ 4 分） | 后端 P6+ 项，占 4 分 | **不在前端 P6+ 考察范围**（前端 P6+ 用 A.1 表，20 分全部为前端项） |
    | 下单幂等 / 并发安全 | 后端 P6+ 各 4 分，端到端验证 | **不计入前端 P6+**；仅要求下单结果正确、不重复、不超卖（属功能正确性），不深究后端实现方案 |
    | 架构说明文档（稳定性 3 分之一） | 需体现 MySQL 分层、数据流、并发决策（若用缓存/Redis 一并说明） | **只需说明整体前后端架构与数据流即可，后端内部架构深度不扣分** |
    | 后端代码质量 / 分层设计 | 纳入观察 | **不作为前端评分依据** |
    
    > **底线不变**：前端候选人仍须交付**完整可运行的全栈项目**（JDK 17 + Spring Boot + 独立 MySQL 齐全并能启动；**Redis 为可选项，用与不用均可**）。放宽的是「后端架构的深度与最优性」，不是「是否需要后端」。若前端候选人后端完全跑不起来、或核心流程因后端不可用而中断，仍按对应功能项归零处理；若候选人声明使用 Redis，则不可用内存 Map 假替。

## Assistant

设计取舍已经收敛为三种候选：微服务（对 200 人规模和交付时限过重）、传统三层单体（实现快但领域边界与并发逻辑易耦合）、模块化单体（推荐）。方案将采用“React SPA + Spring Boot 模块化单体 + MySQL 事实源 + Redis 加速/会话/限流/事件分发”，库存正确性由 MySQL 条件更新兜底，避免把 Redis 锁当作唯一正确性保障。

    # WeBox design documentation
    
    This directory is the review package for the proposed WeBox implementation. It is a design-only deliverable: no application, database, or infrastructure has been initialized yet.
    
    ## Recommended reading order
    
    1. [Requirements and scope](requirements-and-scope.md) — requirement traceability, assumptions, and exclusions
    2. [System architecture](architecture.md) — architecture choice, modules, data flow, reliability, and AI extension seam
    3. [Backend design](backend-design.md) — Spring Boot structure, concurrency, caching, security, and tests
    4. [Frontend design](frontend-design.md) — React suitability, application structure, responsive UX, and tests
    5. [API contract](api.md) — proposed HTTP/SSE endpoints and error conventions
    6. [Database schema](database/schema.sql) — executable MySQL 8 DDL
    7. [Seed data plan](database/seed-data.md) — English demo data and product-image mapping
    8. [Local initialization and delivery](setup-and-delivery.md) — proposed prerequisites, configuration, startup, and delivery checklist
    9. [Development plan](development-plan.md) — phased implementation and acceptance gates
    10. [Decisions for confirmation](decisions-for-confirmation.md) — assumptions requiring owner approval
    
    ## Decision summary
    
    - **Architecture:** modular monolith, not microservices. It preserves clear domain boundaries without creating distributed-system overhead for roughly 200 employees.
    - **Frontend:** React + TypeScript is suitable. It supports a responsive employee SPA and data-heavy Console with one component model and a mature testing ecosystem.
    - **Backend:** Java 17 + Spring Boot, package-by-feature, with application/domain/infrastructure boundaries inside each feature.
    - **Persistence:** MySQL 8 is the system of record. Money is stored as `DECIMAL(10,2)` and calculated with Java `BigDecimal`.
    - **Redis:** Spring Session, hot-menu cache, rate limiting, and stock event fan-out. Redis is an accelerator, not the inventory source of truth.
    - **Inventory:** atomic conditional MySQL updates inside the order transaction prevent overselling. Cancellation restores stock in the same transaction.
    - **Real time:** Server-Sent Events (SSE) push stock changes; reconnect/focus revalidation makes event loss self-healing.
    - **AI readiness:** a provider-neutral recommendation port and SSE contract are reserved, but Tier 3 AI is not in the requested implementation scope.
    - **User-facing language:** English only. Internal code and documentation may use English; this review explanation is in Chinese.

    # System architecture
    
    ## 1. Context and deployment
    
    ```mermaid
    flowchart LR
        U[Employee browser\nMobile or desktop] --> SPA[React + TypeScript SPA]
        A[Admin browser\nConsole] --> SPA
        SPA -->|HTTPS JSON + SSE\nCookie session + CSRF| API[Spring Boot modular monolith]
        API -->|transactions and reports| DB[(MySQL 8\nsource of truth)]
        API -->|sessions, cache, rate limits, Pub/Sub| R[(Redis 7)]
        API -->|images in development| FS[(Local upload directory)]
        API -. future adapter .-> LLM[LLM provider or local model]
    ```
    
    The SPA and API are separate projects during development. Vite proxies `/api` and `/media` to Spring Boot so browser security behavior remains close to same-origin production. A production build can be served by a reverse proxy or Spring static resources; this does not merge the application layers.
    
    ## 2. Backend module boundaries
    
    ```mermaid
    flowchart TB
        WEB[HTTP/SSE adapters]
        ID[Identity]
        CAT[Catalog]
        MENU[Daily Menu]
        PREF[Preferences]
        ORD[Ordering]
        INV[Inventory]
        REP[Reporting]
        REC[Recommendation port]
        INFRA[MySQL / Redis / media adapters]
    
        WEB --> ID
        WEB --> CAT
        WEB --> MENU
        WEB --> PREF
        WEB --> ORD
        WEB --> REP
        WEB -. feature flag .-> REC
        ORD --> MENU
        ORD --> INV
        ORD --> PREF
        REP --> ORD
        REP --> INV
        REC --> MENU
        REC --> PREF
        REC --> ORD
        ID --> INFRA
        CAT --> INFRA
        MENU --> INFRA
        ORD --> INFRA
        INV --> INFRA
    ```
    
    Modules expose application use cases and read models, not repositories. Cross-module calls use explicit interfaces. The database remains one physical schema so order creation, idempotency, uniqueness, inventory deduction, and outbox insertion share one ACID transaction.
    
    ## 3. Critical order flow
    
    ```mermaid
    sequenceDiagram
        participant UI as React checkout
        participant API as Ordering API
        participant DB as MySQL
        participant OUT as Outbox publisher
        participant REDIS as Redis Pub/Sub
        participant SSE as Connected menu clients
    
        UI->>API: POST /orders + Idempotency-Key
        API->>DB: begin transaction
        API->>DB: claim/check idempotency key and request hash
        API->>DB: resolve slot, check active-order constraint
        API->>DB: load authoritative dishes/options and calculate BigDecimal total
        API->>DB: conditional stock decrements (stable item order)
        alt any stock update affects 0 rows
            API->>DB: rollback all changes
            API-->>UI: 409 INVENTORY_INSUFFICIENT + affected dishes
        else stock available
            API->>DB: insert order snapshots + outbox events
            API->>DB: commit
            API-->>UI: 201 order and resolved delivery information
            OUT->>DB: read unpublished events
            OUT->>REDIS: publish stock/dashboard invalidations
            REDIS->>SSE: fan out versioned stock event
            SSE-->>UI: update TanStack Query cache
        end
    ```
    
    The stock statements use `UPDATE daily_menu_item SET remaining_stock = remaining_stock - ?, version = version + 1 WHERE id = ? AND remaining_stock >= ?`. A zero row count is a business conflict. Items are updated by ascending menu-item ID to minimize deadlocks; transient deadlocks are retried a small bounded number of times. Redis locks are deliberately not the correctness boundary: a Redis outage cannot permit overselling.
    
    ## 4. Consistency model
    
    | Data | Authority | Cache/event behavior | Consistency guarantee |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | Users, preferences, dishes, orders | MySQL | Redis entries invalidated after committed outbox event | DB read is authoritative |
    | Menu metadata | MySQL | Redis cache-aside, versioned keys, 5-minute TTL; admin mutations invalidate | Bounded staleness for browse; checkout revalidates |
    | Inventory | MySQL `remaining_stock` | Exact post-commit version/value pushed over SSE; 30-second and focus/reconnect revalidation | Strong at order commit; eventually fresh display |
    | Session | Redis via Spring Session | No database fallback | Consistent shared session; API reports unhealthy if unavailable |
    | Dashboard | MySQL aggregate queries | 15-second cache; order/menu events invalidate | Real order source, near-real-time display |
    | Idempotency | MySQL unique record | Optional short Redis “in progress” optimization only | Durable duplicate prevention |
    
    Cache writes never happen before database commit. Versioned stock events let clients and cache writers ignore out-of-order events. If Redis publication fails, the outbox retains the event for retry. Menu reads can bypass a failed cache; stock/order writes continue as long as MySQL is healthy, while existing login sessions depend on Redis availability.
    
    ## 5. Security and trust boundaries
    
    - Spring Security owns authentication and authorization. Passwords are BCrypt hashes and never logged.
    - Spring Session stores opaque session IDs in Redis. Cookies are `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, and `Secure` outside local development. State-changing requests require a CSRF token.
    - Endpoint rules use `EMPLOYEE` and `ADMIN`; use cases also enforce ownership and role, so URL checks are not the only protection.
    - Request DTOs use Jakarta Bean Validation and explicit maximum lengths. JPA/parameterized SQL prevents SQL injection; user sort fields map through an allowlist.
    - Login and order endpoints use Redis-backed token-bucket limits by IP and authenticated user. A Redis limiter failure uses conservative local limits and emits an alert.
    - Uploads allow JPEG/PNG/WebP, verify decoded MIME type, cap size and dimensions, generate server filenames, and store only relative URLs. Executable/SVG input is rejected.
    - Error responses use stable English codes and safe messages; stack traces and SQL details never cross the API boundary.
    
    ## 6. Reliability and operations
    
    - Flyway owns schema evolution; no `ddl-auto=update`. Startup validates the schema.
    - Liveness reports process state. Readiness checks MySQL and Redis because sessions require Redis.
    - JSON logs include correlation ID, route, latency, status, and non-sensitive user/order identifiers.
    - Metrics cover request latency, cache hit ratio, rate-limit rejection, order outcomes, inventory conflicts, SSE connections, outbox lag, and dashboard query time.
    - Graceful shutdown stops accepting traffic, drains requests, and closes SSE connections with a reconnect hint.
    - Daily reconciliation compares order-item quantities against inventory movements; an `inventory_movement` ledger makes anomalies auditable.
    - Database backups and restore tests are deployment concerns; local development keeps uploaded media under a documented persistent path.
    
    ## 7. AI recommendation extension
    
    The `recommendation` module defines `RecommendationProvider.recommend(RecommendationContext)` and does not depend on a specific LLM SDK. Context contains the English prompt, sanitized preferences, available/non-sold-out menu, allergen exclusions, and dish IDs ordered in the previous seven days.
    
    Safety and correctness remain outside the model:
    
    1. The application removes allergen and sold-out candidates before sending context.
    2. The provider returns structured dish IDs plus reasons, never authoritative dish data.
    3. The application validates returned IDs again and hydrates current cards from the menu module.
    4. `/api/v1/recommendations/stream` uses SSE events (`status`, `recommendation`, `complete`, `error`) so the UI can progressively render cards.
    5. A feature flag hides the entry when no provider/API key is configured. Provider secrets live only in environment variables.
    
    This seam supports Spring AI/OpenAI-compatible APIs or a local model later without changing menu/cart contracts.

    # Requirements and scope
    
    ## 1. Delivery scope
    
    The implementation must cover all Tier 1 and Tier 2 requirements plus the selected Tier 3 capabilities below.
    
    | Area | Required result | Acceptance evidence |
    |---|---|---|
    | Authentication | Register with any valid email, unique email, password policy, login/logout, refresh persistence, role authorization | API/component/E2E tests; employee receives `403` for Console APIs |
    | Daily menu | Responsive cards, details, English data, multi-category filter, name/description search, customization pricing | Employee flow E2E; pagination/performance test |
    | Cart | Quantity/edit/remove, configuration-sensitive lines, exact total, five-serving limit | Unit/component tests and disabled-button UX |
    | Checkout/orders | Slot/date/address, server-side repricing, idempotent submission, history/detail/cancel | Integration tests including duplicate request |
    | Preferences | Allergens, cuisine, spice, taste, budget, preference sorting/highlighting | Allergen confirmation and budget warning E2E |
    | Meal rules | Maximum five servings, cutoff auto-switch, one active order per date/slot | Clock-controlled tests and database constraint test |
    | Console dishes | Search/filter/create/edit/publish state/image upload/daily menu and stock | Admin E2E and authorization tests |
    | Inventory | Remaining stock, low/sold-out states, no oversell, checkout recheck, cancellation restore | Concurrent integration test and two-browser E2E |
    | Dashboard | Today overview, Top 10, meal split, seven-day trend, low-stock list; real order data | Seeded order integration tests and Console E2E |
    | AI extension seam | Provider-neutral context, policy filter, streaming endpoint shape, feature flag | Architecture/API review; no live LLM required in this scope |
    
    All customer-visible strings, including validation and API error messages rendered by the UI, are English. Currency is CNY and displayed with `¥`.
    
    ## 2. Requirement-to-module mapping
    
    | PRD | Frontend feature | Backend module | Main tables/infrastructure |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | 3.1 | `auth` | `identity` | `app_user`, Redis session |
    | 3.2 | `menu` | `catalog`, `menu` | `dish*`, `daily_menu*`, Redis menu cache |
    | 3.3 | `cart` | Checkout validation only | Browser storage; authoritative catalog in MySQL |
    | 3.4 | `checkout`, `orders` | `ordering` | `customer_order*`, `user_address`, `idempotency_record` |
    | 4.1 | `profile` | `preference` | `user_preference`, allergen/category joins |
    | 4.2 | `cart`, `checkout` | `ordering`, `policy` | unique active-slot constraint, injected `Clock` |
    | 4.3 | `console/catalog` | `catalog`, `menu`, `media` | dish/options/menu tables, local media adapter |
    | 5.1 | menu stock UI | `inventory`, `realtime` | atomic stock updates, outbox, Redis Pub/Sub, SSE |
    | 5.2 extension | disabled/flagged AI entry | `recommendation` port | provider adapter seam; optional audit table later |
    | 5.3 | `console/dashboard` | `reporting` | real order queries, short Redis cache |
    
    ## 3. Explicit business interpretations
    
    - A serving is the sum of all cart quantities; option add-ons do not add servings.
    - The maximum of five is checked in the UI and again by the backend.
    - The server resolves cutoff rules in `Asia/Shanghai`; browser time is never authoritative.
    - After a cutoff, the requested slot is normalized to same-day Dinner when available, otherwise next-day Lunch. The response explicitly reports the switch before final placement; the server validates that every cart dish is offered in the resolved menu.
    - An active duplicate means `Pending` or `Confirmed`. `Completed` and `Cancelled` do not block a new order. The database enforces this rule in addition to the application check.
    - Order count on the dashboard includes orders created for today's delivery date, including cancelled orders. Revenue excludes cancelled orders. This is called out for confirmation.
    - Dashboard “today” is based on delivery date, not record creation time.
    - Cart state is local to the signed-in browser and namespaced by user ID. Cross-device cart synchronization is not required by the PRD. Every value is revalidated/repriced on the server.
    - Cancelling is permitted only from `Pending`. It atomically changes status and restores inventory once.
    - Dish deletion is not exposed. “Unpublish” preserves order history and referential integrity.
    
    ## 4. Scope boundaries
    
    The detailed Tier 2 requirements specify dish/menu management, not a Console order-processing workflow. The architecture reserves order status commands, but a full operations queue is not part of the mandatory implementation unless confirmed otherwise. Payment, email verification, delivery routing, refunds, multilingual UI, and live LLM integration are also outside this scope.
    
    ## 5. Non-functional targets
    
    | Quality | Initial target |
    |---|---|
    | Menu read | p95 < 300 ms locally with warm cache; first useful UI < 2 s on a typical laptop |
    | Order placement | p95 < 800 ms locally excluding client network; exactly one result per idempotency key |
    | Capacity | 200 employees, burst-oriented 09:30–10:00 traffic; design target 50 order requests/s locally |
    | Availability behavior | MySQL failure fails closed; Redis cache/Pub/Sub failure degrades to DB reads and periodic refresh; session Redis failure requires explicit service recovery |
    | Security | BCrypt, server session cookie, CSRF, validation, prepared queries, role checks, upload validation, rate limits |
    | Accessibility | Keyboard navigation, labelled forms, semantic controls, visible focus, status not conveyed by color alone |
    | Observability | Structured logs with request/user/order IDs, Actuator health/metrics, cache and order outcome metrics |
    
    ## 6. Architecture alternatives considered
    
    1. **Recommended: modular monolith.** Fast local operation and transactions, while feature packages keep ownership explicit. It can later split reporting or recommendations behind existing ports.
    2. **Simple controller-service-repository monolith.** Lowest initial code volume, but catalog, menu, ordering, and inventory policies tend to leak into a large service layer and become harder to test.
    3. **Microservices.** Independent scaling is unnecessary for this load and would add deployment, distributed transaction, tracing, and contract overhead without business value.

    # Backend design
    
    ## 1. Technology baseline
    
    | Concern | Choice | Reason |
    |---|---|---|
    | Runtime | Java 17, Spring Boot 3.x | Required runtime, mature production ecosystem |
    | Web/security | Spring MVC, Spring Security, Spring Session Data Redis | Straightforward REST/SSE and server-controlled sessions |
    | Persistence | Spring Data JPA for aggregates; JdbcTemplate for atomic inventory/report queries | Maintainability plus explicit SQL where correctness/performance matters |
    | Schema | MySQL 8, Flyway | Independent durable database and repeatable migrations |
    | Redis | Lettuce/Spring Data Redis | Cache, session, Pub/Sub, and rate-limit primitives |
    | API docs | springdoc-openapi | Executable contract and Swagger UI in local profile |
    | Mapping/validation | Manual mappers, Jakarta Bean Validation | Explicit boundary behavior without entity leakage |
    | Tests | JUnit 5, AssertJ, MockMvc, Testcontainers MySQL/Redis | Production-equivalent integration behavior; no H2 |
    | Observability | Actuator, Micrometer, structured Logback JSON | Health, metrics, and traceable operations |
    
    Dependency versions will be pinned in the build and upgraded through reviewed pull requests. Maven Wrapper is committed so the only host build prerequisite is JDK 17 plus Docker or local MySQL/Redis.
    
    ## 2. Project and package layout
    
    ```text
    backend/
    ├── pom.xml
    ├── src/main/java/com/webox/
    │   ├── WeboxApplication.java
    │   ├── shared/                 # errors, money, clock, security context, paging
    │   ├── identity/
    │   │   ├── api/                # controllers and request/response DTOs
    │   │   ├── application/        # RegisterUser, Login handled by Security, CurrentUser
    │   │   ├── domain/
    │   │   └── infrastructure/
    │   ├── catalog/                # dishes, allergens, option groups/values, media
    │   ├── menu/                   # dated menus and employee read model
    │   ├── preference/
    │   ├── ordering/               # quote, place, list/detail, cancel, policy
    │   ├── inventory/              # conditional updates, movements, events
    │   ├── reporting/              # dashboard projections
    │   ├── recommendation/         # disabled port and future provider adapter
    │   └── platform/               # Redis, outbox, rate limit, OpenAPI, config
    ├── src/main/resources/
    │   ├── application.yml
    │   ├── application-local.yml
    │   └── db/migration/
    └── src/test/
    ```
    
    Each feature uses `api → application → domain`, with infrastructure implementing domain/application ports. Controllers never expose JPA entities. Repositories are package-private where practical. Architecture tests (ArchUnit) forbid API/domain dependencies on infrastructure and cross-feature repository access.
    
    ## 3. Domain responsibilities
    
    | Module | Owns | Does not own |
    |---|---|---|
    | `identity` | registration, credentials, role, current principal | profile food preferences |
    | `catalog` | dish lifecycle, allergens, option definitions, image metadata | dated availability/stock |
    | `menu` | date publication, offered dishes, initial stock, browse queries | decrement correctness |
    | `preference` | allergen/cuisine/taste/spice/budget settings | LLM logic |
    | `ordering` | cutoff normalization, quote, idempotency, active-order rule, snapshots, state transition | cache implementation |
    | `inventory` | atomic decrement/restore, movement ledger, stock events | cart quantities before checkout |
    | `reporting` | read-only aggregates and DTOs | duplicated reporting tables initially |
    | `recommendation` | context assembly/provider port/policy post-filter | authoritative menu or allergy data |
    
    ## 4. Transaction design
    
    ### Place order
    
    One `@Transactional` application use case performs the following:
    
    1. Validate the `Idempotency-Key` format and claim `(user_id, key)` with a unique insert. Store a SHA-256 hash of the canonical request.
    2. If the key exists with the same hash and a completed order, return the saved response; with a different hash, return `409 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED`.
    3. Normalize date/meal slot using an injected `Clock` and `Asia/Shanghai` rules.
    4. Check for an existing active order. The generated-column unique index is the final race-condition guard.
    5. Load current dish/menu/option rows and reject unpublished, unavailable, or invalid selections.
    6. Calculate unit and total prices using `BigDecimal`; client totals are display hints only.
    7. Aggregate quantities by `daily_menu_item`, sort IDs, and perform conditional stock updates.
    8. Insert order and immutable item/option snapshots, inventory movements, and outbox rows.
    9. Complete the idempotency row with order ID/status, then commit.
    
    Expected unique-constraint races and insufficient inventory are translated into deterministic `409` responses. Unexpected exceptions roll back the idempotency claim so a safe retry can proceed. A request in progress returns `409 REQUEST_IN_PROGRESS` with `Retry-After` rather than starting parallel work.
    
    ### Cancel order
    
    Lock or conditionally update the owned order from `PENDING` to `CANCELLED`. Only when one row changes, restore each menu item, append `CANCEL_RESTORE` movements, and enqueue events in the same transaction. Repeated cancel calls return the already-cancelled representation and never restore twice.
    
    ## 5. Pricing and validation
    
    - MySQL uses `DECIMAL(10,2)`; Java uses `BigDecimal` with scale 2 and `RoundingMode.UNNECESSARY` for persisted prices.
    - Option values contribute a fixed `price_delta`. An item unit price is dish base price plus selected deltas; subtotal is unit price × quantity.
    - Required groups must have exactly one selection. Single-select groups accept at most one. Multi-select groups de-duplicate values.
    - All selected values must belong to the chosen dish and be active.
    - Order snapshots preserve dish/option names and prices even after catalog edits.
    - Search term ≤ 50, email/address ≤ 200, names/descriptions/options have limits reflected in DTO and DDL.
    
    ## 6. Cutoff policy
    
    `MealSlotResolver` is a pure, clock-driven component:
    
    ```text
    requested future slot                 -> unchanged
    today Lunch before 10:00              -> today Lunch
    today Lunch at/after 10:00 before 15:00 -> today Dinner
    today Lunch/Dinner at/after 15:00      -> tomorrow Lunch
    today Dinner before 15:00             -> today Dinner
    past date                              -> validation error
    ```
    
    Cutoff is exclusive: exactly `10:00:00` closes Lunch and exactly `15:00:00` closes Dinner. The quote endpoint returns the resolved slot and warnings before `Place Order`; the order endpoint repeats resolution to prevent time-of-check/time-of-use errors.
    
    ## 7. Cache and real-time behavior
    
    ### Menu cache
    
    - Key: `webox:menu:v{schemaVersion}:{date}:{publicationVersion}`.
    - Value: normalized English dish/menu metadata. Query-specific filtering and pagination occur over the bounded daily set to avoid a combinatorial key space.
    - TTL: five minutes plus ±10% jitter. Admin dish/menu changes bump the date publication version after commit.
    - Inventory values in responses are merged from current menu-item rows or exact versioned stock cache entries; checkout always uses MySQL.
    - A short Redis `SET NX PX` rebuild lease prevents stampedes. Callers that miss the lease read MySQL instead of waiting indefinitely.
    
    ### Events and SSE
    
    - The transactional outbox publisher claims rows using `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, publishes versioned events, and records publication time.
    - Redis channel is an internal fan-out transport; browser clients connect to `/api/v1/menu/stock-stream?date=...`.
    - SSE sends `stock.changed`, heartbeat, and `resync.required`. It never exposes internal entity payloads.
    - On initial connect/reconnect the UI fetches a fresh menu page; it also revalidates on window focus and every 30 seconds.
    
    ### Rate limits
    
    Redis Lua performs atomic token-bucket updates. Proposed defaults are 10 login attempts/10 minutes/IP, 10 order attempts/minute/user, and 120 general API requests/minute/user. Values are configuration, not hard-coded policy. `429` includes `Retry-After`.
    
    ## 8. Dashboard queries
    
    The reporting repository uses explicit, indexed SQL and maps directly to immutable DTOs:
    
    - overview groups by status for a delivery date; revenue sums non-cancelled order totals;
    - Top 10 sums item quantities for non-cancelled orders and uses item snapshot names;
    - meal split counts non-cancelled orders by `meal_slot`;
    - trend groups delivery date for the inclusive last seven dates;
    - low stock reads today's published menu where remaining stock ≤ 3.
    
    Results cache for 15 seconds and are invalidated by order placed/cancelled/status-changed and menu-stock events. The endpoint also supports `refresh=true` for the Console refresh button, restricted and rate-limited.
    
    ## 9. Error contract and status codes
    
    Spring `ProblemDetail` is extended with `code`, `correlationId`, `fieldErrors`, and optional `details`. Messages are English.
    
    | Condition | HTTP | Code |
    |---|---:|---|
    | Invalid input | 400 | `VALIDATION_FAILED` |
    | Missing/invalid session | 401 | `AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED` |
    | Wrong role/ownership | 403 | `ACCESS_DENIED` |
    | Missing resource | 404 | `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND` |
    | Active order exists | 409 | `ACTIVE_ORDER_EXISTS` |
    | Idempotency conflict/in progress | 409 | `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED` / `REQUEST_IN_PROGRESS` |
    | Inventory conflict | 409 | `INVENTORY_INSUFFICIENT` |
    | Business cutoff/menu mismatch | 422 | `ORDER_SLOT_ADJUSTED` or `MENU_ITEM_UNAVAILABLE` |
    | Rate limited | 429 | `RATE_LIMITED` |
    
    The quote endpoint normally handles slot adjustment as a successful response with a warning. `ORDER_SLOT_ADJUSTED` is reserved for a placement request whose resolved slot changed after the quote and requires explicit user review.
    
    ## 10. Test strategy
    
    - **Unit:** cutoff boundaries, five-serving rule, price calculations, required options, preference ranking, allergen matching.
    - **Application:** use cases with fake clock and ports; authorization and ownership paths.
    - **Integration:** Testcontainers MySQL/Redis; Flyway migration, repository SQL, cache invalidation, outbox, session, idempotency.
    - **Concurrency:** 20+ threads order the last N units; assert successful quantity equals N, stock is zero, movement ledger balances, and no duplicate active order exists.
    - **API:** MockMvc/OpenAPI-compatible request/response and English errors.
    - **Failure:** Redis cache unavailable falls back to DB; outbox retry; deadlock retry; session outage readiness failure.
    
    Add: /Users/aixuankai/codes/github/webox-coding-test/docs/frontend-design.md
    # Frontend design
    
    ## 1. Is React + TypeScript suitable?
    
    Yes. The product combines a card-oriented employee experience, complex checkout state, a data-heavy admin Console, responsive mobile/desktop layouts, real-time SSE updates, and charts. React's component ecosystem, TanStack Query cache model, mature form/testing libraries, and broad hiring familiarity fit this mix well. TypeScript protects API and money/state boundaries.
    
    Vue 3 would also be a sound choice and can be more concise for a small team, while Next.js would add server-rendering and server/runtime concepts that provide little value for this authenticated internal SPA. The recommended stack is therefore **React + TypeScript + Vite**, without SSR.
    
    ## 2. Proposed stack
    
    | Concern | Choice |
    |---|---|
    | Build/runtime | Vite, React, strict TypeScript |
    | Routing | React Router with employee/admin route trees |
    | Server state | TanStack Query; generated or typed API client from OpenAPI |
    | Local cart/UI state | Zustand with per-user persisted cart and schema version |
    | Forms | React Hook Form + Zod; server errors mapped by field |
    | UI foundation | Ant Design components/tokens plus scoped CSS for responsive employee cards |
    | Charts | Apache ECharts through a small adapter component |
    | Money | Integer fen in UI computations/formatting; API decimal strings parsed at boundary |
    | Tests | Vitest, React Testing Library, MSW, Playwright |
    
    Ant Design is particularly effective for the Console tables/forms. Employee pages use the same accessible primitives but a dedicated responsive shell, not a desktop Console squeezed onto mobile.
    
    ## 3. Source layout
    
    ```text
    frontend/
    ├── src/
    │   ├── app/               # router, providers, auth bootstrap, error boundary
    │   ├── api/               # client, generated types, ProblemDetail mapping, SSE
    │   ├── shared/            # UI primitives, money/date helpers, constants
    │   ├── features/
    │   │   ├── auth/
    │   │   ├── menu/
    │   │   ├── cart/
    │   │   ├── checkout/
    │   │   ├── orders/
    │   │   ├── preferences/
    │   │   ├── console-catalog/
    │   │   ├── console-menu/
    │   │   ├── console-dashboard/
    │   │   └── recommendation/ # feature-flagged extension boundary
    │   ├── layouts/           # EmployeeLayout and ConsoleLayout
    │   └── main.tsx
    ├── e2e/
    └── public/
    ```
    
    Feature folders own page, components, hooks, schema, and tests. Shared code must be domain-neutral. API decimal strings never flow directly into arithmetic; date-only values remain `YYYY-MM-DD` and are not converted through UTC timestamps.
    
    ## 4. Routes and access
    
    ```text
    /login, /register                         public
    /menu, /dishes/:id, /cart, /checkout     employee
    /orders, /orders/:id, /settings           employee
    /console/dishes, /console/menus           admin
    /console/dashboard                        admin
    ```
    
    The app loads `/auth/me` before protected rendering. Client guards improve UX but are not security boundaries; the API repeats authorization. A `403` displays an English access page, and a `401` clears user-scoped query/cart state before redirecting.
    
    ## 5. Employee experience and responsiveness
    
    - Mobile uses a compact top bar and bottom navigation, a one-column menu, sticky cart summary, full-width drawers/modals, and touch targets of at least 44 px.
    - Tablet uses two columns; desktop uses three/four depending on container width and a persistent cart affordance.
    - Menu filter chips support multiple categories. Search is limited to 50 characters and debounced about 250 ms; in-flight obsolete queries are cancelled.
    - Results are server-paged and retain previous data during transitions. Card images have fixed aspect ratio, lazy loading, responsive sources, and skeleton placeholders.
    - Dish detail requires all mandatory option groups before Add to Cart. The displayed price updates from integer fen.
    - At five servings, all relevant add buttons disable with `Maximum 5 items per order.` This is immediate UX; server enforcement remains final.
    - Rapid repeated Add to Cart clicks are serialized per card for 300 ms while still allowing intentional quantity changes in the cart. Functional state updates prevent lost increments.
    
    ## 6. Preferences and warnings
    
    The cart add command compares dish allergens against the current profile. A match opens an English confirmation listing exact allergens. Confirming adds the configured line; cancelling changes nothing. This is never a hidden filter.
    
    When “Recommended for me” is on, the API returns a deterministic preference score and match reasons; the UI sorts/highlights those dishes. Spice and cuisine affect this score. Taste intensity is stored and displayed but reserved primarily for the future AI provider. Checkout compares total against the budget maximum and shows a non-blocking warning.
    
    ## 7. Cart and checkout state
    
    A cart-line identity is a stable hash of `dishId` plus sorted option-value IDs. Thus equal configurations merge and different configurations remain separate. Persistence includes a schema version and user ID; logout removes sensitive/query state and makes another user's cart inaccessible.
    
    Checkout flow:
    
    1. User selects address/date/slot and the UI requests `/orders/quote`.
    2. The response replaces client prices with authoritative values, reports budget/inventory/menu issues, an existing order, and any slot switch.
    3. User explicitly accepts a changed slot and submits with a UUID `Idempotency-Key` retained until a definitive result.
    4. UI disables submission while pending. A network timeout retries with the same key; success clears only the placed lines.
    5. Inventory conflict highlights affected lines and returns the user to an editable cart.
    
    ## 8. Real-time stock
    
    An authenticated `EventSource`-compatible SSE client subscribes for the visible date (using a fetch-based SSE client if CSRF/header behavior requires it). Events update only newer `stockVersion` values in TanStack Query caches. Cards show `Only N left` for 1–3 and `Sold out` for 0; controls become disabled.
    
    SSE is advisory. Initial page load, reconnection, window focus, and a 30-second interval revalidate the menu. A connection badge is unnecessary unless disconnection persists; the UI can quietly fall back to refresh and show a small English notice.
    
    ## 9. Console design
    
    - Desktop Console uses side navigation; narrow screens collapse it to a drawer.
    - Dish table supports debounced search, category filter, server pagination, edit, and publish toggle.
    - The dish form handles English metadata, fixed-enum allergens/spice/category, nested option groups/values, image preview, upload progress, and server validation.
    - Daily Menu defaults to tomorrow but permits today. It shows publish status and editable stock per dish; destructive stock reductions below sold quantity are rejected by the API.
    - Dashboard cards/charts share one response timestamp, expose a `Refresh` button, and auto-refresh every 30 seconds while visible. Charts include labels/tooltips and a table/text fallback for accessibility.
    
    ## 10. Frontend score coverage
    
    | Appendix A.1 criterion | Design evidence |
    |---|---|
    | Responsive | Separate employee/Console shells, 1/2/3–4 column layout, mobile navigation |
    | Pagination/list performance | Server pagination, debounced/cancelled search, lazy images, stable cache; virtualization can be added beyond 200 visible rows |
    | Debounce/throttle | Search debounce, per-card add serialization, disabled submit with durable idempotency key |
    | Price precision | Decimal string boundary and integer-fen UI calculations; backend reprice |
    | Input validation | Shared Zod/RHF constraints, length limits, safe rendering, server validation mapping |
    
    ## 11. Frontend tests
    
    - Unit/component tests cover cart identity, five-item limit, exact totals, required options, allergen dialog, budget warning, role guards, and stock event ordering.
    - MSW integration tests cover ProblemDetail errors, slot changes, idempotent timeout/retry, and inventory conflicts.
    - Playwright runs employee registration → menu → configured cart → checkout → order detail/cancel, and admin login → dish/menu edit → dashboard.
    - Responsive E2E runs at representative mobile and desktop viewports; accessibility smoke tests use axe.
    

## Assistant

前后端主设计已落到文档中。关键点包括：服务端会重新计价和校验选项；同一日期/餐次的有效订单由应用逻辑与数据库唯一约束双重保证；库存扣减、订单快照、幂等记录和事件出箱在同一事务中完成；前端用 SSE 更新库存，但下单时始终以 MySQL 为准。接下来补齐 API、可执行 DDL、种子数据方案与初始化说明。
