    Add: /Users/aixuankai/codes/github/webox-coding-test/docs/api.md
    # Proposed API contract
    
    Base path is `/api/v1`. JSON uses `camelCase`, date-only values use ISO `YYYY-MM-DD`, instants use ISO-8601 with an offset, and money is a two-decimal string such as `"22.50"`. All user-facing messages are English.
    
    ## 1. Common behavior
    
    - Authentication uses an `HttpOnly` session cookie. Browser requests include credentials.
    - `GET /auth/csrf` returns/sets the CSRF token required on state-changing requests.
    - List endpoints return `{items, page, size, totalItems, totalPages}`. Default/max sizes are 20/100.
    - State-changing requests accept `X-Correlation-Id`; the server creates one if absent.
    - Order placement requires `Idempotency-Key: <UUID>`.
    - Errors use `application/problem+json`:
    
    ```json
    {
      "type": "https://webox.local/problems/inventory-insufficient",
      "title": "Inventory is insufficient",
      "status": 409,
      "detail": "Some dishes no longer have the requested quantity.",
      "code": "INVENTORY_INSUFFICIENT",
      "correlationId": "01J...",
      "details": {
        "items": [{"dishId": 9, "name": "Classic Beef Burger", "requested": 2, "remaining": 1}]
      }
    }
    ```
    
    ## 2. Authentication and current user
    
    | Method | Path | Access | Purpose |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | GET | `/auth/csrf` | public | Bootstrap CSRF token |
    | POST | `/auth/register` | public | Register employee and establish session |
    | POST | `/auth/login` | public | Establish session |
    | POST | `/auth/logout` | signed in | Invalidate session |
    | GET | `/auth/me` | signed in | Current ID, email, role, display name |
    
    Registration accepts `email`, `password`, and `displayName`; password is 8–72 characters with at least one ASCII letter and digit. Login errors intentionally use a generic `Invalid email or password.` response.
    
    ## 3. Menu, dishes, and stock
    
    | Method | Path | Access | Purpose |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | GET | `/menu?date=&q=&categories=&page=&size=&recommended=` | employee | Published daily menu with stock and preference metadata |
    | GET | `/menu/dishes/{dishId}?date=` | employee | Dish details and active customization options |
    | GET | `/menu/stock-stream?date=` | employee | SSE stock updates |
    
    Menu items include `menuItemId`, `dish`, `basePrice`, `remainingStock`, `stockVersion`, `lowStock`, `soldOut`, and optional `preferenceMatches`. `q` is at most 50 characters; categories are allowlisted enums.
    
    SSE event example:
    
    ```text
    event: stock.changed
    id: 18442
    data: {"date":"2026-10-24","menuItemId":9,"remainingStock":2,"stockVersion":7}
    ```
    
    ## 4. Preferences and addresses
    
    | Method | Path | Access | Purpose |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | GET | `/preferences` | employee | Get dietary preferences |
    | PUT | `/preferences` | employee | Replace validated preference set |
    | GET | `/addresses` | employee | List saved addresses |
    | POST | `/addresses` | employee | Add address |
    | PATCH | `/addresses/{id}` | owner | Edit/default address |
    | DELETE | `/addresses/{id}` | owner | Soft-delete address |
    
    Preference payload contains `allergens`, `preferredCategories`, `spiceLevel`, `tasteIntensity`, `budgetMin`, and `budgetMax`. Budget values are nullable decimal strings and min must not exceed max.
    
    ## 5. Quote and orders
    
    | Method | Path | Access | Purpose |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | POST | `/orders/quote` | employee | Reprice and validate cart; resolve slot |
    | POST | `/orders` | employee | Idempotently place order |
    | GET | `/orders?page=&size=&status=` | employee | Own order history |
    | GET | `/orders/{orderId}` | owner/admin | Order details and snapshots |
    | POST | `/orders/{orderId}/cancel` | owner | Cancel a Pending order and restore stock |
    
    Quote/place request shape:
    
    ```json
    {
      "deliveryDate": "2026-10-24",
      "mealSlot": "LUNCH",
      "addressId": 12,
      "newAddress": null,
      "acceptedResolvedSlot": null,
      "items": [
        {"dishId": 4, "quantity": 2, "optionValueIds": [8, 11]}
      ]
    }
    ```
    
    The quote response contains authoritative line prices, `total`, `resolvedDeliveryDate`, `resolvedMealSlot`, `slotAdjusted`, warnings (`BUDGET_EXCEEDED`, etc.), unavailable items, and optional `existingOrder`. When a new address is supplied, it is validated and saved only as part of successful placement if `saveAddress=true`.
    
    `POST /orders` returns `201` for the first completed key and `200` when replaying the completed same request/key. A reused key with a different request returns `409`. An active-order conflict includes its order ID so the UI can offer `View Existing Order`.
    
    ## 6. Console catalog and menus
    
    All endpoints require `ADMIN`.
    
    | Method | Path | Purpose |
    |---|---|---|
    | GET | `/console/dishes?q=&category=&published=&page=&size=` | Search/filter all dishes |
    | POST | `/console/dishes` | Create English dish and option definitions |
    | GET | `/console/dishes/{id}` | Edit model |
    | PUT | `/console/dishes/{id}` | Replace editable data with optimistic version |
    | PATCH | `/console/dishes/{id}/publication` | Publish/unpublish |
    | POST | `/console/media/images` | Validated image upload; returns relative media URL |
    | GET | `/console/menus/{date}` | Dated menu editor model |
    | PUT | `/console/menus/{date}` | Create/update offered dishes and initial/current stock |
    | POST | `/console/menus/{date}/publish` | Publish menu |
    
    Dish writes contain `version`; stale edits return `409 OPTIMISTIC_LOCK_CONFLICT`. Menu stock cannot be reduced below already sold net quantity. Removing an offered item with order references is disallowed; it can be hidden from new browsing instead.
    
    ## 7. Console dashboard
    
    | Method | Path | Access | Purpose |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | GET | `/console/dashboard?date=&refresh=` | admin | One consistent dashboard snapshot |
    
    Response shape:
    
    ```json
    {
      "asOf": "2026-10-24T11:05:00+08:00",
      "date": "2026-10-24",
      "overview": {"totalOrders": 42, "revenue": "1188.50", "completed": 10, "pending": 20, "confirmed": 9, "cancelled": 3},
      "topDishes": [{"dishId": 1, "name": "Kung Pao Chicken", "quantity": 21}],
      "mealDistribution": [{"mealSlot": "LUNCH", "orders": 36}, {"mealSlot": "DINNER", "orders": 3}],
      "trend": [{"date": "2026-10-18", "orders": 31, "revenue": "850.00"}],
      "lowStock": [{"dishId": 9, "name": "Classic Beef Burger", "remainingStock": 2}]
    }
    ```
    
    ## 8. Reserved AI extension
    
    `POST /recommendations/stream` is reserved behind `features.aiRecommendation`. It accepts `{prompt, date, mealSlot}` and returns SSE `status`, `recommendation`, `complete`, or `error` events. Recommendation events contain validated current dish cards and an English reason. When disabled the endpoint returns `404 FEATURE_NOT_AVAILABLE`, avoiding a non-functional UI control.
    
    Add: /Users/aixuankai/codes/github/webox-coding-test/docs/database/schema.sql
    -- WeBox MySQL 8 schema proposal. Flyway will split/version this during implementation.
    CREATE DATABASE IF NOT EXISTS webox
      CHARACTER SET utf8mb4
      COLLATE utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;
    USE webox;
    
    CREATE TABLE app_user (
      id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
      email VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL,
      password_hash VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
      display_name VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
      role VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
      enabled BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,
      created_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      updated_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3) ON UPDATE CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      UNIQUE KEY uk_app_user_email (email),
      CONSTRAINT ck_app_user_role CHECK (role IN ('EMPLOYEE', 'ADMIN'))
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE user_preference (
      user_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      spice_level VARCHAR(20) NULL,
      taste_intensity VARCHAR(20) NULL,
      budget_min DECIMAL(10,2) NULL,
      budget_max DECIMAL(10,2) NULL,
      created_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      updated_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3) ON UPDATE CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      PRIMARY KEY (user_id),
      CONSTRAINT fk_preference_user FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES app_user (id),
      CONSTRAINT ck_preference_spice CHECK (spice_level IS NULL OR spice_level IN ('NONE', 'MILD', 'MEDIUM', 'HOT')),
      CONSTRAINT ck_preference_taste CHECK (taste_intensity IS NULL OR taste_intensity IN ('LIGHT', 'BALANCED', 'RICH')),
      CONSTRAINT ck_preference_budget CHECK (
        (budget_min IS NULL OR budget_min >= 0) AND
        (budget_max IS NULL OR budget_max >= 0) AND
        (budget_min IS NULL OR budget_max IS NULL OR budget_min <= budget_max)
      )
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE user_allergen (
      user_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      allergen VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
      PRIMARY KEY (user_id, allergen),
      CONSTRAINT fk_user_allergen_user FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES app_user (id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
      CONSTRAINT ck_user_allergen CHECK (allergen IN ('PEANUTS', 'DAIRY', 'EGG', 'GLUTEN', 'SOY', 'FISH', 'SHELLFISH'))
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE user_preferred_category (
      user_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      category VARCHAR(30) NOT NULL,
      PRIMARY KEY (user_id, category),
      CONSTRAINT fk_user_category_user FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES app_user (id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
      CONSTRAINT ck_user_category CHECK (category IN ('CHINESE', 'WESTERN', 'JAPANESE', 'LIGHT_MEAL', 'KOREAN', 'SOUTHEAST_ASIAN'))
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE user_address (
      id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
      user_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      label VARCHAR(50) NULL,
      address_text VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL,
      is_default BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
      deleted_at DATETIME(3) NULL,
      created_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      updated_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3) ON UPDATE CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      KEY idx_address_user_active (user_id, deleted_at),
      CONSTRAINT fk_address_user FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES app_user (id)
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE dish (
      id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
      name VARCHAR(120) NOT NULL,
      description VARCHAR(1000) NOT NULL,
      base_price DECIMAL(10,2) NOT NULL,
      category VARCHAR(30) NOT NULL,
      protein_source VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
      spice_level VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
      image_url VARCHAR(500) NOT NULL,
      published BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,
      version BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
      created_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      updated_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3) ON UPDATE CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      KEY idx_dish_filter (published, category, name),
      CONSTRAINT ck_dish_price CHECK (base_price >= 0),
      CONSTRAINT ck_dish_category CHECK (category IN ('CHINESE', 'WESTERN', 'JAPANESE', 'LIGHT_MEAL', 'KOREAN', 'SOUTHEAST_ASIAN')),
      CONSTRAINT ck_dish_spice CHECK (spice_level IN ('NONE', 'MILD', 'MEDIUM', 'HOT'))
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE dish_allergen (
      dish_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      allergen VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
      PRIMARY KEY (dish_id, allergen),
      CONSTRAINT fk_dish_allergen_dish FOREIGN KEY (dish_id) REFERENCES dish (id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
      CONSTRAINT ck_dish_allergen CHECK (allergen IN ('PEANUTS', 'DAIRY', 'EGG', 'GLUTEN', 'SOY', 'FISH', 'SHELLFISH'))
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE dish_option_group (
      id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
      dish_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      name VARCHAR(80) NOT NULL,
      selection_type VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
      required_selection BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
      min_selections SMALLINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
      max_selections SMALLINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,
      display_order SMALLINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
      active BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      KEY idx_option_group_dish (dish_id, active, display_order),
      CONSTRAINT fk_option_group_dish FOREIGN KEY (dish_id) REFERENCES dish (id),
      CONSTRAINT ck_option_group_type CHECK (selection_type IN ('SINGLE', 'MULTIPLE')),
      CONSTRAINT ck_option_group_range CHECK (min_selections <= max_selections AND max_selections <= 20),
      CONSTRAINT ck_option_group_required CHECK (required_selection = FALSE OR min_selections >= 1)
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE dish_option_value (
      id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
      option_group_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      name VARCHAR(80) NOT NULL,
      price_delta DECIMAL(10,2) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0.00,
      display_order SMALLINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
      active BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      UNIQUE KEY uk_option_value_name (option_group_id, name),
      KEY idx_option_value_group (option_group_id, active, display_order),
      CONSTRAINT fk_option_value_group FOREIGN KEY (option_group_id) REFERENCES dish_option_group (id),
      CONSTRAINT ck_option_value_price CHECK (price_delta >= 0)
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE daily_menu (
      id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
      menu_date DATE NOT NULL,
      status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'DRAFT',
      publication_version BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
      published_at DATETIME(3) NULL,
      created_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      updated_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3) ON UPDATE CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      UNIQUE KEY uk_daily_menu_date (menu_date),
      CONSTRAINT ck_daily_menu_status CHECK (status IN ('DRAFT', 'PUBLISHED'))
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE daily_menu_item (
      id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
      daily_menu_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      dish_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      initial_stock INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      remaining_stock INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      visible BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,
      stock_version BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
      created_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      updated_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3) ON UPDATE CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      UNIQUE KEY uk_daily_menu_dish (daily_menu_id, dish_id),
      KEY idx_menu_item_stock (daily_menu_id, remaining_stock, visible),
      CONSTRAINT fk_menu_item_menu FOREIGN KEY (daily_menu_id) REFERENCES daily_menu (id),
      CONSTRAINT fk_menu_item_dish FOREIGN KEY (dish_id) REFERENCES dish (id),
      CONSTRAINT ck_menu_item_stock CHECK (remaining_stock <= initial_stock)
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE customer_order (
      id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
      order_number VARCHAR(32) NOT NULL,
      user_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      delivery_date DATE NOT NULL,
      meal_slot VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
      delivery_address VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL,
      status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
      total_amount DECIMAL(10,2) NOT NULL,
      active_slot_guard TINYINT AS (CASE WHEN status IN ('PENDING', 'CONFIRMED') THEN 1 ELSE NULL END) STORED,
      version BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
      created_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      updated_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3) ON UPDATE CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      cancelled_at DATETIME(3) NULL,
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      UNIQUE KEY uk_order_number (order_number),
      UNIQUE KEY uk_active_order_slot (user_id, delivery_date, meal_slot, active_slot_guard),
      KEY idx_order_user_created (user_id, created_at DESC),
      KEY idx_order_dashboard (delivery_date, status, meal_slot),
      CONSTRAINT fk_order_user FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES app_user (id),
      CONSTRAINT ck_order_slot CHECK (meal_slot IN ('LUNCH', 'DINNER')),
      CONSTRAINT ck_order_status CHECK (status IN ('PENDING', 'CONFIRMED', 'COMPLETED', 'CANCELLED')),
      CONSTRAINT ck_order_total CHECK (total_amount >= 0)
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE order_item (
      id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
      order_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      daily_menu_item_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      dish_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      dish_name_snapshot VARCHAR(120) NOT NULL,
      base_price_snapshot DECIMAL(10,2) NOT NULL,
      unit_price DECIMAL(10,2) NOT NULL,
      quantity SMALLINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      subtotal DECIMAL(10,2) NOT NULL,
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      KEY idx_order_item_order (order_id),
      KEY idx_order_item_dish_reporting (dish_id, order_id),
      CONSTRAINT fk_order_item_order FOREIGN KEY (order_id) REFERENCES customer_order (id),
      CONSTRAINT fk_order_item_menu_item FOREIGN KEY (daily_menu_item_id) REFERENCES daily_menu_item (id),
      CONSTRAINT fk_order_item_dish FOREIGN KEY (dish_id) REFERENCES dish (id),
      CONSTRAINT ck_order_item_quantity CHECK (quantity BETWEEN 1 AND 5),
      CONSTRAINT ck_order_item_money CHECK (base_price_snapshot >= 0 AND unit_price >= 0 AND subtotal >= 0)
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE order_item_option (
      id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
      order_item_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      option_group_name_snapshot VARCHAR(80) NOT NULL,
      option_value_name_snapshot VARCHAR(80) NOT NULL,
      price_delta_snapshot DECIMAL(10,2) NOT NULL,
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      KEY idx_order_item_option_item (order_item_id),
      CONSTRAINT fk_order_option_item FOREIGN KEY (order_item_id) REFERENCES order_item (id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
      CONSTRAINT ck_order_option_price CHECK (price_delta_snapshot >= 0)
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE idempotency_record (
      id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
      user_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      idempotency_key CHAR(36) NOT NULL,
      request_hash CHAR(64) NOT NULL,
      state VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
      order_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NULL,
      response_status SMALLINT UNSIGNED NULL,
      created_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      completed_at DATETIME(3) NULL,
      expires_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL,
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      UNIQUE KEY uk_idempotency_user_key (user_id, idempotency_key),
      KEY idx_idempotency_expiry (expires_at),
      CONSTRAINT fk_idempotency_user FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES app_user (id),
      CONSTRAINT fk_idempotency_order FOREIGN KEY (order_id) REFERENCES customer_order (id),
      CONSTRAINT ck_idempotency_state CHECK (state IN ('PROCESSING', 'COMPLETED'))
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE inventory_movement (
      id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
      daily_menu_item_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      order_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      movement_type VARCHAR(30) NOT NULL,
      quantity_delta INT NOT NULL,
      stock_after INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      created_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      UNIQUE KEY uk_inventory_order_type_item (order_id, movement_type, daily_menu_item_id),
      KEY idx_inventory_menu_time (daily_menu_item_id, created_at),
      CONSTRAINT fk_inventory_menu_item FOREIGN KEY (daily_menu_item_id) REFERENCES daily_menu_item (id),
      CONSTRAINT fk_inventory_order FOREIGN KEY (order_id) REFERENCES customer_order (id),
      CONSTRAINT ck_inventory_type CHECK (movement_type IN ('ORDER_DEDUCT', 'CANCEL_RESTORE')),
      CONSTRAINT ck_inventory_delta CHECK (quantity_delta <> 0)
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE outbox_event (
      id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
      aggregate_type VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
      aggregate_id VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
      event_type VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
      payload JSON NOT NULL,
      occurred_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      published_at DATETIME(3) NULL,
      attempt_count INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
      next_attempt_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      KEY idx_outbox_unpublished (published_at, next_attempt_at, id)
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;

    Add: /Users/aixuankai/codes/github/webox-coding-test/docs/database/seed-data.md
    # Seed data and product images
    
    `seed.sql` provides the nine English dishes from the PRD, their fixed allergens, all documented customization groups, and published menus for both the run date and following date. Each item starts with 20 servings, so the demo can exercise ordering, low stock, sold-out behavior, cancellation restore, and dashboard changes.
    
    The supplied source directory is:
    
    ```text
    ~/Downloads/WeBox-PRD-候选人交付包/product_images/
    ```
    
    During implementation, visually appropriate files will be copied (not referenced by an absolute machine path) into a versioned seed-media directory and renamed to stable URLs:
    
    ```text
    image_01_s-*.jpg -> image_01.jpg -> /media/seed/image_01.jpg
    ...
    image_09_s-*.jpg -> image_09.jpg -> /media/seed/image_09.jpg
    ```
    
    The current numeric mapping in `seed.sql` is provisional until the images are visually matched to dishes. All 20 supplied images remain available for selecting the best nine. Copyright/source metadata is not included in the delivery package; production use requires confirming asset rights.
    
    Demo users are created by an idempotent local-profile bootstrap component, not raw SQL, because passwords must be BCrypt-hashed and must not be committed. Proposed local defaults are documented through `.env.example`, while real passwords remain environment variables. This bootstrap never runs in production profiles.

    -- The local-profile bootstrap command will BCrypt-hash passwords supplied through

    # Local initialization and delivery plan
    
    This document describes the target repository after implementation. At the current design-review stage only `docs/` exists; the commands become actionable after approval and scaffolding.
    
    ## 1. Prerequisites
    
    - JDK 17
    - Node.js active LTS and npm
    - MySQL 8 as an independent local process, reachable as `root` with no password per the requested local environment
    - Redis 7 as an independent local process
    - Maven Wrapper and npm lockfile committed by the project
    
    Local no-password root access is accepted only as the requested development default. Staging/production must use a least-privilege `webox_app` account and secrets supplied outside Git.
    
    ## 2. Proposed repository layout
    
    ```text
    webox-coding-test/
    ├── README.md
    ├── backend/
    ├── frontend/
    ├── docs/
    ├── ai-conversations/          # complete native AI conversation exports
    ├── data/uploads/              # gitignored local uploads
    ├── .env.example
    └── compose.yml                # optional convenience, still independent MySQL/Redis processes
    ```
    
    ## 3. Database and Redis initialization
    
    For the requested local MySQL account:
    
    ```bash
    mysql -u root < docs/database/schema.sql
    mysql -u root < docs/database/seed.sql
    redis-cli ping
    ```
    
    The application default configuration is equivalent to:
    
    ```text
    DB_URL=jdbc:mysql://localhost:3306/webox?useUnicode=true&characterEncoding=utf8&serverTimezone=Asia/Shanghai
    DB_USERNAME=root
    DB_PASSWORD=
    REDIS_HOST=localhost
    REDIS_PORT=6379
    APP_TIME_ZONE=Asia/Shanghai
    ```
    
    In the implemented project, Flyway runs the versioned equivalent of `schema.sql`; `seed.sql` becomes a local/dev repeatable seed migration or an explicit seed command, not an unconditional production migration. `spring.jpa.hibernate.ddl-auto=validate` prevents accidental schema drift.
    
    ## 4. Target startup
    
    ```bash
    cd backend
    ./mvnw spring-boot:run -Dspring-boot.run.profiles=local
    ```
    
    ```bash
    cd frontend
    npm ci
    npm run dev
    ```
    
    The final root README will include exact URLs, demo-account creation, health checks, Swagger/OpenAPI location, seed/reset procedure, and troubleshooting for occupied ports, MySQL authentication, Redis availability, and timezone mismatch.
    
    ## 5. Configuration contract
    
    `.env.example` documents only non-secret examples. Required production values fail startup with a clear message. Planned properties include database/Redis endpoints, session cookie security, allowed origin, upload path/limits, rate-limit buckets, menu/dashboard cache TTLs, outbox batch/retry settings, feature flags, and optional LLM provider/model/key.
    
    AI recommendation defaults to disabled. Supplying a key alone does not expose it; both provider configuration and the feature flag are required.
    
    ## 6. Verification commands after implementation
    
    ```bash
    cd backend && ./mvnw verify
    cd frontend && npm run lint && npm run type-check && npm run test -- --run
    cd frontend && npm run e2e
    ```
    
    Backend verification uses Testcontainers MySQL/Redis and never substitutes H2. An additional concurrency test runs against MySQL 8 to prove no overselling and idempotent order creation.
    
    ## 7. Required delivery artifacts
    
    - Runnable frontend and backend with locked dependencies
    - Root README from zero to running application
    - OpenAPI document plus this human-readable API summary
    - Architecture and data-flow documentation
    - Executable automated tests and test commands
    - MySQL migration and English demo seed data for today's menu
    - Complete original AI-tool conversation export under `ai-conversations/`
    - `.env.example`, no real secrets or API keys
    - Brief demo script: employee order, second-browser stock update, cancellation restore, admin menu edit, dashboard refresh
    
    ## 8. Operational notes
    
    - Do not reset or reseed a shared environment. The local reset command will require an explicit `local` profile and confirmation.
    - Uploaded files live outside the source tree in runtime environments and require backup alongside the database until an object-storage adapter is introduced.
    - Database schema changes are forward migrations. Destructive migrations require a backup/rollback plan.
    - Before handoff, scan Git history and AI conversation exports for credentials and redact only secrets while retaining the complete conversation content required by the PRD.

    # Decisions for confirmation
    
    The design is implementable with the recommended defaults below. Approval of the design without exceptions will be treated as approval of these defaults.
    
    | # | Decision | Recommended default | Impact if changed |
    |---:|---|---|---|
    | 1 | Dashboard definition | “Today” means delivery date; total orders includes cancelled, revenue and rankings exclude cancelled | Changes report SQL and acceptance fixtures only |
    | 2 | Cutoff auto-switch UX | Quote shows the new date/slot and requires explicit acknowledgement before placement | Silent switching is faster but risks surprising users |
    | 3 | Console order processing | Not mandatory because Tier 2 details only dish/menu management; reserve status commands for later | Adding a queue and Confirm/Complete actions expands UI/API/tests |
    | 4 | Cart persistence | Per-user browser-local cart, no cross-device synchronization | Server cart adds tables/APIs/concurrency and cleanup work |
    | 5 | Authentication | Redis-backed server session + CSRF, not browser-stored JWT | JWT changes token refresh/revocation and browser security model |
    | 6 | Product images | Visually select and copy the best nine supplied JPEGs into stable project seed media during implementation | The provisional numeric mapping should not be relied on yet |
    | 7 | Local infrastructure | Support the requested local root/no-password MySQL and local Redis; optional Compose as convenience | Compose-only would conflict with an existing local service workflow |
    | 8 | Admin demo account | Local-profile bootstrap from environment passwords; no credential/hash embedded in SQL | A committed fixed demo password is simpler but weaker hygiene |
    | 9 | AI | Build the extension port/feature flag only; do not integrate an LLM in the required scope | Live AI requires provider/key/model and adds failure/security testing |

    ## Approval gate
    
    Please confirm the design and the defaults above, or list the decision numbers to change. No frontend/backend scaffolding, database creation, or seed-asset copying will be performed before that confirmation.

    # Development plan and acceptance gates
    
    Implementation begins only after the design and open decisions are approved. The plan prioritizes complete vertical flows over disconnected screens.
    
    ## Phase 0 — Foundations
    
    Deliver:
    
    - Maven/Spring Boot and Vite/React strict-TypeScript projects
    - environment profiles, Flyway, MySQL/Redis connectivity, health checks
    - shared error contract, OpenAPI, logging/correlation ID
    - frontend application shells, API client, design tokens, responsive baseline
    - CI commands and Testcontainers foundation
    
    Gate: clean database migrates, both projects start from README, health/readiness are meaningful, and no secret is committed.
    
    ## Phase 1 — Identity and authorization
    
    Deliver registration/login/logout/current-user, Redis session, CSRF, BCrypt, validation, role routes, admin bootstrap, and rate limits.
    
    Gate: refresh keeps login; duplicate email and invalid credentials show English feedback; employee access to Console UI/API is denied; integration and E2E tests pass.
    
    ## Phase 2 — Catalog, daily menu, and Console management
    
    Deliver dish/options/allergens model, media upload, admin CRUD/publication, dated menu/stock editor, English seed data for today/tomorrow, employee menu/details/filter/search/pagination, and cache-aside behavior.
    
    Gate: admin changes are visible after cache invalidation; unpublished dishes are absent; required options and exact display prices work; mobile/desktop layouts pass.
    
    ## Phase 3 — Preferences and cart
    
    Deliver settings, recommendation toggle deterministic ranking, allergen confirmation, configuration-aware local cart, quantity controls, five-serving UX, exact money handling, and budget warning.
    
    Gate: component tests cover all warning/limit paths; different configurations remain separate lines; malicious client totals cannot affect later server quote.
    
    ## Phase 4 — Ordering and business rules
    
    Deliver addresses, quote, cutoff resolver, server repricing, active-order uniqueness, durable idempotency, history/detail/cancel, immutable snapshots, and English errors.
    
    Gate: registration-to-order and history/cancel E2E pass; exact cutoff tests pass; same-key retry returns one order; concurrent requests cannot create two active same-slot orders.
    
    ## Phase 5 — Inventory and real-time updates
    
    Deliver atomic deduction/restore, movement ledger, insufficient-item response, outbox publisher, Redis Pub/Sub, SSE, low/sold-out UI, reconnect revalidation, and cache consistency behavior.
    
    Gate: concurrent last-stock test proves no negative stock/oversell; failed multi-item deduction rolls back every item; cancellation restores exactly once; a second browser updates without manual refresh.
    
    ## Phase 6 — Dashboard
    
    Deliver real-order aggregates, 15-second Redis cache/invalidation, overview, Top 10 bar chart, meal distribution, seven-day order/revenue trend, low-stock list, manual and 30-second refresh.
    
    Gate: integration fixtures prove all numbers and cancellation semantics; Console E2E renders empty/loading/error/populated states; data changes after a real order.
    
    ## Phase 7 — Hardening and handoff
    
    Deliver failure-path tests, ArchUnit rules, accessibility/responsive checks, query/index review, cache/limiter metrics, final OpenAPI, root README, demo script, and AI conversation exports.
    
    Gate: backend `verify`, frontend lint/type/test, and Playwright all pass from a clean checkout; fresh local startup follows only documented steps; delivery checklist is complete.
    
    ## Optional Phase 8 — AI implementation (not currently requested)
    
    After mandatory acceptance, configure a real provider, implement structured streaming, policy pre/post-filters, seven-day exclusion, card hydration, provider timeout/circuit breaker, prompt-injection defenses, and live-provider tests behind a separate profile.
    
    ## Test coverage matrix
    
    | Risk | Unit | Integration | E2E/manual |
    |---|---:|---:|---:|
    | Password/roles/session | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
    | Price/options/five servings | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
    | Cutoff normalization | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
    | Active-order uniqueness | ✓ | ✓ concurrent | ✓ |
    | Idempotency | ✓ | ✓ concurrent | timeout/retry ✓ |
    | No overselling/rollback | ✓ | ✓ concurrent | two-browser ✓ |
    | Cache consistency |  | ✓ with Redis | admin-to-menu ✓ |
    | SSE stock freshness | ✓ reducer | ✓ publisher | two-browser ✓ |
    | Dashboard correctness | ✓ | ✓ SQL fixtures | ✓ |
    | Responsive/accessibility | ✓ component |  | mobile/desktop + axe |
    
    ## Definition of done for every feature
    
    - Frontend and backend path is complete; no mock data remains in production paths.
    - Authorization, input limits, English success/error/empty/loading states, and observability are included.
    - Tests demonstrate the business rule at the lowest useful level plus one end-to-end happy path.
    - API/schema/documentation changes ship with code in the same review.
    - Cache invalidation and failure behavior are explicit for every cached write path.

    ---
    name: verification-before-completion
    description: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always
    ---
    
    # Verification Before Completion
    
    ## Overview
    
    **Core principle:** Evidence before claims, always.
    
    **Violating the letter of this rule is violating the spirit of this rule.**
    
    ## The Iron Law
    
    ```
    NO COMPLETION CLAIMS WITHOUT FRESH VERIFICATION EVIDENCE
    ```
    
    If you haven't run the verification command in this message, you cannot claim it passes.
    
    ## The Gate Function
    
    ```
    BEFORE claiming any status or expressing satisfaction:
    
    1. IDENTIFY: What command proves this claim?
    2. RUN: Execute the FULL command (fresh, complete)
    3. READ: Full output, check exit code, count failures
    4. VERIFY: Does output confirm the claim?
       - If NO: State actual status with evidence
       - If YES: State claim WITH evidence
    5. ONLY THEN: Make the claim
    
    Skip any step = lying, not verifying
    ```
    
    ## Common Failures
    
    | Claim | Requires | Not Sufficient |
    |-------|----------|----------------|
    | Tests pass | Test command output: 0 failures | Previous run, "should pass" |
    | Linter clean | Linter output: 0 errors | Partial check, extrapolation |
    | Build succeeds | Build command: exit 0 | Linter passing, logs look good |
    | Bug fixed | Test original symptom: passes | Code changed, assumed fixed |
    | Regression test works | Red-green cycle verified | Test passes once |
    | Agent completed | VCS diff shows changes | Agent reports "success" |
    | Requirements met | Line-by-line checklist | Tests passing |
    
    ## Red Flags - STOP
    
    - Using "should", "probably", "seems to"
    - Expressing satisfaction before verification ("Great!", "Perfect!", "Done!", etc.)
    - About to commit/push/PR without verification
    - Trusting agent success reports
    - Relying on partial verification
    - Thinking "just this once"
    - Tired and wanting work over
    - **ANY wording implying success without having run verification**
    
    ## Rationalization Prevention
    
    | Excuse | Reality |
    |--------|---------|
    | "Should work now" | RUN the verification |
    | "I'm confident" | Confidence ≠ evidence |
    | "Just this once" | No exceptions |
    | "Linter passed" | Linter ≠ compiler |
    | "Agent said success" | Verify independently |
    | "I'm tired" | Exhaustion ≠ excuse |
    | "Partial check is enough" | Partial proves nothing |
    | "Different words so rule doesn't apply" | Spirit over letter |
    
    ## Key Patterns
    
    **Tests:**
    ```
    ✅ [Run test command] [See: 34/34 pass] "All tests pass"
    ❌ "Should pass now" / "Looks correct"
    ```
    
    **Regression tests (TDD Red-Green):**
    ```
    ✅ Write → Run (pass) → Revert fix → Run (MUST FAIL) → Restore → Run (pass)
    ❌ "I've written a regression test" (without red-green verification)
    ```
    
    **Build:**
    ```
    ✅ [Run build] [See: exit 0] "Build passes"
    ❌ "Linter passed" (linter doesn't check compilation)
    ```
    
    **Requirements:**
    ```
    ✅ Re-read plan → Create checklist → Verify each → Report gaps or completion
    ❌ "Tests pass, phase complete"
    ```
    
    **Agent delegation:**
    ```
    ✅ Agent reports success → Check VCS diff → Verify changes → Report actual state
    ❌ Trust agent report
    ```
    
    ## When To Apply
    
    **ALWAYS before:**
    - ANY variation of success/completion claims
    - ANY expression of satisfaction
    - ANY positive statement about work state
    - Committing, PR creation, task completion
    - Moving to next task
    - Delegating to agents
    
    **Rule applies to:**
    - Exact phrases
    - Paraphrases and synonyms
    - Implications of success
    - ANY communication suggesting completion/correctness

    ---
    name: brainstorming
    description: "You MUST use this before any creative work - creating features, building components, adding functionality, or modifying behavior. Explores user intent, requirements and design before implementation."
    ---
    
    # Brainstorming Ideas Into Designs
    
    Help turn ideas into fully formed designs and specs through natural collaborative dialogue.
    
    Start by understanding the current project context, then ask questions one at a time to refine the idea. Once you understand what you're building, present the design and get user approval.
    
    <HARD-GATE>
    Do NOT invoke any implementation skill, write any code, scaffold any project, or take any implementation action until you have presented a design and the user has approved it. This applies to EVERY project regardless of perceived simplicity.
    </HARD-GATE>
    
    ## Anti-Pattern: "This Is Too Simple To Need A Design"
    
    Every project goes through this process. A todo list, a single-function utility, a config change — all of them. "Simple" projects are where unexamined assumptions cause the most wasted work. The design can be short (a few sentences for truly simple projects), but you MUST present it and get approval.
    
    ## Checklist
    
    You MUST create a task for each of these items and complete them in order:
    
    1. **Explore project context** — check files, docs, recent commits
    2. **Offer visual companion** (if topic will involve visual questions) — this is its own message, not combined with a clarifying question. See the Visual Companion section below.
    3. **Ask clarifying questions** — one at a time, understand purpose/constraints/success criteria
    4. **Propose 2-3 approaches** — with trade-offs and your recommendation
    5. **Present design** — in sections scaled to their complexity, get user approval after each section
    6. **Write design doc** — save to `docs/superpowers/specs/YYYY-MM-DD-<topic>-design.md` and commit
    7. **Spec self-review** — quick inline check for placeholders, contradictions, ambiguity, scope (see below)
    8. **User reviews written spec** — ask user to review the spec file before proceeding
    9. **Transition to implementation** — invoke writing-plans skill to create implementation plan
    
    ## Process Flow
    
    ```dot
    digraph brainstorming {
        "Explore project context" [shape=box];
        "Visual questions ahead?" [shape=diamond];
        "Offer Visual Companion\n(own message, no other content)" [shape=box];
        "Ask clarifying questions" [shape=box];
        "Propose 2-3 approaches" [shape=box];
        "Present design sections" [shape=box];
        "User approves design?" [shape=diamond];
        "Write design doc" [shape=box];
        "Spec self-review\n(fix inline)" [shape=box];
        "User reviews spec?" [shape=diamond];
        "Invoke writing-plans skill" [shape=doublecircle];
    
        "Explore project context" -> "Visual questions ahead?";
        "Visual questions ahead?" -> "Offer Visual Companion\n(own message, no other content)" [label="yes"];
        "Visual questions ahead?" -> "Ask clarifying questions" [label="no"];
        "Offer Visual Companion\n(own message, no other content)" -> "Ask clarifying questions";
        "Ask clarifying questions" -> "Propose 2-3 approaches";
        "Propose 2-3 approaches" -> "Present design sections";
        "Present design sections" -> "User approves design?";
        "User approves design?" -> "Present design sections" [label="no, revise"];
        "User approves design?" -> "Write design doc" [label="yes"];
        "Write design doc" -> "Spec self-review\n(fix inline)";
        "Spec self-review\n(fix inline)" -> "User reviews spec?";
        "User reviews spec?" -> "Write design doc" [label="changes requested"];
        "User reviews spec?" -> "Invoke writing-plans skill" [label="approved"];
    }
    ```
    
    **The terminal state is invoking writing-plans.** Do NOT invoke frontend-design, mcp-builder, or any other implementation skill. The ONLY skill you invoke after brainstorming is writing-plans.
    
    ## The Process
    
    **Understanding the idea:**
    
    - Check out the current project state first (files, docs, recent commits)
    - Before asking detailed questions, assess scope: if the request describes multiple independent subsystems (e.g., "build a platform with chat, file storage, billing, and analytics"), flag this immediately. Don't spend questions refining details of a project that needs to be decomposed first.
    - If the project is too large for a single spec, help the user decompose into sub-projects: what are the independent pieces, how do they relate, what order should they be built? Then brainstorm the first sub-project through the normal design flow. Each sub-project gets its own spec → plan → implementation cycle.
    - For appropriately-scoped projects, ask questions one at a time to refine the idea
    - Prefer multiple choice questions when possible, but open-ended is fine too
    - Only one question per message - if a topic needs more exploration, break it into multiple questions
    - Focus on understanding: purpose, constraints, success criteria
    
    **Exploring approaches:**
    
    - Propose 2-3 different approaches with trade-offs
    - Present options conversationally with your recommendation and reasoning
    - Lead with your recommended option and explain why
    
    **Presenting the design:**
    
    - Once you believe you understand what you're building, present the design
    - Scale each section to its complexity: a few sentences if straightforward, up to 200-300 words if nuanced
    - Ask after each section whether it looks right so far
    - Cover: architecture, components, data flow, error handling, testing
    - Be ready to go back and clarify if something doesn't make sense
    
    **Design for isolation and clarity:**
    
    - Break the system into smaller units that each have one clear purpose, communicate through well-defined interfaces, and can be understood and tested independently
    - For each unit, you should be able to answer: what does it do, how do you use it, and what does it depend on?
    - Can someone understand what a unit does without reading its internals? Can you change the internals without breaking consumers? If not, the boundaries need work.
    - Smaller, well-bounded units are also easier for you to work with - you reason better about code you can hold in context at once, and your edits are more reliable when files are focused. When a file grows large, that's often a signal that it's doing too much.
    
    **Working in existing codebases:**
    
    - Explore the current structure before proposing changes. Follow existing patterns.
    - Where existing code has problems that affect the work (e.g., a file that's grown too large, unclear boundaries, tangled responsibilities), include targeted improvements as part of the design - the way a good developer improves code they're working in.
    - Don't propose unrelated refactoring. Stay focused on what serves the current goal.
    
    ## After the Design
    
    **Documentation:**
    
    - Write the validated design (spec) to `docs/superpowers/specs/YYYY-MM-DD-<topic>-design.md`
      - (User preferences for spec location override this default)
    - Use elements-of-style:writing-clearly-and-concisely skill if available
    - Commit the design document to git
    
    **Spec Self-Review:**
    After writing the spec document, look at it with fresh eyes:
    
    1. **Placeholder scan:** Any "TBD", "TODO", incomplete sections, or vague requirements? Fix them.
    2. **Internal consistency:** Do any sections contradict each other? Does the architecture match the feature descriptions?
    3. **Scope check:** Is this focused enough for a single implementation plan, or does it need decomposition?
    4. **Ambiguity check:** Could any requirement be interpreted two different ways? If so, pick one and make it explicit.
    
    Fix any issues inline. No need to re-review — just fix and move on.
    
    **User Review Gate:**
    After the spec review loop passes, ask the user to review the written spec before proceeding:
    
    > "Spec written and committed to `<path>`. Please review it and let me know if you want to make any changes before we start writing out the implementation plan."
    
    Wait for the user's response. If they request changes, make them and re-run the spec review loop. Only proceed once the user approves.
    
    **Implementation:**
    
    - Invoke the writing-plans skill to create a detailed implementation plan
    - Do NOT invoke any other skill. writing-plans is the next step.
    
    ## Key Principles
    
    - **One question at a time** - Don't overwhelm with multiple questions
    - **Multiple choice preferred** - Easier to answer than open-ended when possible
    - **YAGNI ruthlessly** - Remove unnecessary features from all designs
    - **Explore alternatives** - Always propose 2-3 approaches before settling
    - **Incremental validation** - Present design, get approval before moving on
    - **Be flexible** - Go back and clarify when something doesn't make sense
    
    ## Visual Companion
    
    A browser-based companion for showing mockups, diagrams, and visual options during brainstorming. Available as a tool — not a mode. Accepting the companion means it's available for questions that benefit from visual treatment; it does NOT mean every question goes through the browser.
    
    **Offering the companion:** When you anticipate that upcoming questions will involve visual content (mockups, layouts, diagrams), offer it once for consent:
    > "Some of what we're working on might be easier to explain if I can show it to you in a web browser. I can put together mockups, diagrams, comparisons, and other visuals as we go. This feature is still new and can be token-intensive. Want to try it? (Requires opening a local URL)"
    
    **This offer MUST be its own message.** Do not combine it with clarifying questions, context summaries, or any other content. The message should contain ONLY the offer above and nothing else. Wait for the user's response before continuing. If they decline, proceed with text-only brainstorming.
    
    **Per-question decision:** Even after the user accepts, decide FOR EACH QUESTION whether to use the browser or the terminal. The test: **would the user understand this better by seeing it than reading it?**
    
    - **Use the browser** for content that IS visual — mockups, wireframes, layout comparisons, architecture diagrams, side-by-side visual designs
    - **Use the terminal** for content that is text — requirements questions, conceptual choices, tradeoff lists, A/B/C/D text options, scope decisions
    
    A question about a UI topic is not automatically a visual question. "What does personality mean in this context?" is a conceptual question — use the terminal. "Which wizard layout works better?" is a visual question — use the browser.
    
    If they agree to the companion, read the detailed guide before proceeding:
    `skills/brainstorming/visual-companion.md`

    docs/architecture.md:55:Modules expose application use cases and read models, not repositories. Cross-module calls use explicit interfaces. The database remains one physical schema so order creation, idempotency, uniqueness, inventory deduction, and outbox insertion share one ACID transaction.
    docs/architecture.md:65:    participant REDIS as Redis Pub/Sub
    docs/architecture.md:88:The stock statements use `UPDATE daily_menu_item SET remaining_stock = remaining_stock - ?, version = version + 1 WHERE id = ? AND remaining_stock >= ?`. A zero row count is a business conflict. Items are updated by ascending menu-item ID to minimize deadlocks; transient deadlocks are retried a small bounded number of times. Redis locks are deliberately not the correctness boundary: a Redis outage cannot permit overselling.
    docs/architecture.md:94:| Users, preferences, dishes, orders | MySQL | Redis entries invalidated after committed outbox event | DB read is authoritative |
    docs/architecture.md:95:| Menu metadata | MySQL | Redis cache-aside, versioned keys, 5-minute TTL; admin mutations invalidate | Bounded staleness for browse; checkout revalidates |
    docs/architecture.md:97:| Session | Redis via Spring Session | No database fallback | Consistent shared session; API reports unhealthy if unavailable |
    docs/architecture.md:99:| Idempotency | MySQL unique record | Optional short Redis “in progress” optimization only | Durable duplicate prevention |
    docs/architecture.md:101:Cache writes never happen before database commit. Versioned stock events let clients and cache writers ignore out-of-order events. If Redis publication fails, the outbox retains the event for retry. Menu reads can bypass a failed cache; stock/order writes continue as long as MySQL is healthy, while existing login sessions depend on Redis availability.
    docs/architecture.md:106:- Spring Session stores opaque session IDs in Redis. Cookies are `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, and `Secure` outside local development. State-changing requests require a CSRF token.
    docs/architecture.md:109:- Login and order endpoints use Redis-backed token-bucket limits by IP and authenticated user. A Redis limiter failure uses conservative local limits and emits an alert.
    docs/architecture.md:116:- Liveness reports process state. Readiness checks MySQL and Redis because sessions require Redis.
    docs/architecture.md:117:- JSON logs include correlation ID, route, latency, status, and non-sensitive user/order identifiers.
    docs/architecture.md:118:- Metrics cover request latency, cache hit ratio, rate-limit rejection, order outcomes, inventory conflicts, SSE connections, outbox lag, and dashboard query time.
    docs/architecture.md:120:- Daily reconciliation compares order-item quantities against inventory movements; an `inventory_movement` ledger makes anomalies auditable.

## Assistant

库存方案有三种可选路径：MySQL 同步扣减最稳；Redis 分布式锁包住 MySQL 仍然同步、收益有限；Redis Lua 预占后由 Stream 消费者异步落 MySQL吞吐最好，但必须把“订单成功”放在 MySQL 确认之后。方案将采用第三种的安全版本：允许接口先返回 `202 Processing`，只有 MySQL 幂等落库成功后才进入 `Pending` 并展示下单成功；超时、重复消费、Redis 数据丢失都有补偿或对账路径。

    -The stock statements use `UPDATE daily_menu_item SET remaining_stock = remaining_stock - ?, version = version + 1 WHERE id = ? AND remaining_stock >= ?`. A zero row count is a business conflict. Items are updated by ascending menu-item ID to minimize deadlocks; transient deadlocks are retried a small bounded number of times. Redis locks are deliberately not the correctness boundary: a Redis outage cannot permit overselling.
    +All stock keys for a delivery date use one Redis Cluster hash tag so a Lua script can check every requested dish, decrement them, persist a reservation record, and append its Redis Stream event atomically. A consumer group performs MySQL conditional updates in stable item-ID order. It creates the order only after every update succeeds, and uses the reservation/idempotency ID as a unique deduplication key.
    +
    +The asynchronous boundary is internal: **a Redis reservation alone is never an accepted order**. The API waits briefly for durable confirmation; on timeout it returns `202 Processing`, and the frontend polls/retries the submission with the same idempotency key. Only a MySQL-confirmed order returns `201` and appears as `Pending`.
     
    +Failure handling is explicit:
    +
    +- consumer crash before commit leaves the Stream entry pending for redelivery;
    +- crash after commit but before acknowledgement is harmless because the MySQL deduplication key returns the existing order;
    +- a terminal MySQL conflict triggers an idempotent Redis compensation and a failed submission;
    +- Redis loss before MySQL confirmation cannot produce a false success;
    +- Redis loss after MySQL confirmation is repaired from MySQL by reconciliation, while the existing order remains durable;
    +- scheduled reconciliation compares Redis values, `daily_menu_item`, and the movement ledger, alerts on drift, and rebuilds only from MySQL-confirmed state.
    +
    +Redis uses AOF and a `noeviction` policy for reservation/Stream keys. MySQL still uses `UPDATE ... WHERE remaining_stock >= ?` as the last invariant, so Redis split-brain or stale initialization fails safely rather than overselling.
    +

    -## 5. Pricing and validation
    +| Actor | From | To | Inventory effect |
    +|---|---|---|---|
    +| Employee | `PENDING` | `CANCELLED` | Restore exactly once |
    +| Admin | `PENDING` | `CONFIRMED` | None |
    +| Admin | `PENDING` | `CANCELLED` | Restore exactly once |
    +| Admin | `CONFIRMED` | `COMPLETED` | None |
    +| Admin | `CONFIRMED` | `CANCELLED` | Restore exactly once |
    +
    +`COMPLETED` and `CANCELLED` are terminal. Restoration is committed in MySQL first and emitted through the outbox to Redis; a temporarily stale Redis value is conservative (too low), and versioned reconciliation repairs it. The unique inventory movement prevents a repeated transition from restoring twice. No operator queue is required.
    +
    +## 5. Authentication and actor context
    +
    +- Access JWT lifetime defaults to 15 minutes. It contains subject/user ID, role, issuer, audience, issued-at, expiry, and token ID; no profile or preference data.
    +- The SPA stores access JWT only in memory and sends `Authorization: Bearer ...`. It never stores either token in `localStorage`.
    +- Refresh is an opaque 256-bit value in a path-scoped `HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `SameSite` cookie. Redis stores only an HMAC/hash, user ID, family ID, generation, expiry, and revoked flag.
    +- `/api/v1/auth/refresh` rotates token and cookie in one atomic Redis Lua operation. Old-token reuse revokes the family and emits a security event.
    +- `JwtAuthenticationFilter` builds `AuthenticatedActor`; `SecurityFilterChain` applies App/Admin prefixes. `@PreAuthorize` and domain checks enforce ownership and transition rights.
    +- Key IDs support signing-key rotation. Clock skew is bounded; issuer/audience/algorithm are fixed, never accepted from untrusted headers.

    +
    +- Keys use `{inventory:YYYY-MM-DD}` hash tags so every item in an order is in one Redis Cluster slot.

    +

    +
    +Transition payload is `{targetStatus, expectedVersion, reason}`. Allowed transitions are `PENDING → CONFIRMED`, `PENDING → CANCELLED`, `CONFIRMED → COMPLETED`, and `CONFIRMED → CANCELLED`. Terminal states reject changes with `409 INVALID_ORDER_TRANSITION`; a stale version returns `409 OPTIMISTIC_LOCK_CONFLICT`.     # 附录 A 评分预评估
    
    ## 1. 结论
    
    在本方案全部正确实现并通过验收的前提下：
    
    - Tier 1 + Tier 2 目标为 **50 / 50**，超过 45 分功能门槛。
    - Tier 3 实现库存实时扣减和经营看板，不实现 AI。附录只公布 Tier 3 总计 `+10`，没有公布三个子项权重，因此准确加分只能表示为 `库存权重 + 看板权重`。若按常见拆分估计，约为 **+6～+8 / 10**，该区间不是正式评分规则。
    - 前端岗位 P6+ 设计覆盖 **20 / 20**。
    - 后端岗位 P6+ 设计覆盖 **20 / 20**。
    - 仅就附录 A 已公布的“功能 60 + P6+ 20”口径，完整实现后的预期目标约为 **76～78 / 80**。若三个 Tier 3 功能等权，则约为 `50 + 6.67 + 20 = 76.67 / 80`。
    
    这不是当前实得分。当前仓库只有设计文档，没有运行代码、测试和端到端证据；按严格面试评分，功能与 P6+ 均暂时不可计分。
    
    ## 2. 功能完成度
    
    | 项目 | 目标 | 方案依据 | 风险 |
    |---|---:|---|---|
    | Tier 1 | 30 / 30 | 注册登录、菜单、购物车、结算、幂等下单、订单历史/取消均有前后端/API/表设计 | 必须用 E2E 证明完整链路，局部页面不能得分 |
    | Tier 2 | 20 / 20 | 偏好、过敏原提醒、推荐排序、预算、订餐规则、Console 菜品/每日菜单均覆盖 | 时间边界、五份限制、有效订单唯一约束需自动化证明 |
    | Tier 3 库存 | 权重未公开 | Redis Lua 预占、Stream、MySQL 条件确认、补偿、对账、SSE | 成功点若错误地放在 Redis 预占后，该项及依赖项可能级联归零 |
    | Tier 3 看板 | 权重未公开 | 所有指标来自真实订单/MySQL 聚合，支持缓存失效和刷新 | 必须以真实下单数据演示，静态数据不计分 |
    | Tier 3 AI | 0 | 只保留扩展口，不实际接入 LLM | 不获得 AI 加分，但不影响 Tier 1/2 门槛 |
    
    新增的 Admin 订单状态机完善了产品背景中的“处理订单”：`Pending → Confirmed → Completed`，以及管理员取消路径。它提高端到端完整性，但附录没有给它独立分值，不重复加分。
    
    ## 3. 前端岗位 P6+（A.1）
    
    | 考察点 | 设计覆盖 | 分数目标 | 验收证据 |
    |---|---|---:|---|
    | 多屏幕适配 | 独立员工端/Console 布局、移动底栏、响应式网格、抽屉式后台导航 | 4 / 4 | 手机和桌面 Playwright + axe |
    | 分页/列表性能 | 服务端分页、Query 缓存、搜索取消、图片懒加载、稳定占位 | 4 / 4 | 大数据集响应/渲染测试 |
    | 防抖/节流 | 搜索防抖、Add 操作串行化、提交禁用、同一幂等键重试 | 4 / 4 | 频繁点击组件/E2E 测试 |
    | 价格精度 | API decimal string，前端整数分，后端 `BigDecimal` 重算 | 4 / 4 | 加料、数量、总价边界测试 |
    | 输入校验 | Zod/RHF、长度限制、枚举、服务端字段错误映射 | 4 / 4 | 表单和恶意输入测试 |
    | **合计** |  | **20 / 20** | 必须实现后才能计入实得分 |
    
    React + TypeScript 与本场景匹配：员工端需要复杂交互与响应式组件，Console 需要表格、表单和图表；使用同一类型系统和 OpenAPI 客户端能减少两端契约漂移。
    
    ## 4. 后端岗位 P6+（A.2）
    
    | 考察点 | 设计覆盖 | 分数目标 | 验收证据 |
    |---|---|---:|---|
    | 缓存策略 | 菜单 cache-aside、版本键/TTL/抖动/失效；看板短缓存；库存不把缓存值当最终成功 | 4 / 4 | 命中率、失效、Redis 故障测试 |
    | 下单幂等 | MySQL 唯一键、请求哈希、Redis reservation ID、Stream 重投去重、完成结果重放 | 4 / 4 | 重复点击、超时重试、重复消息测试 |
    | SQL 注入防护 | 参数化查询/JPA、排序字段白名单、输入上限 | 4 / 4 | 注入用例和代码检查 |
    | 用户权限校验 | App/Admin URI 分离；一个 JWT Filter 创建统一 Actor；FilterChain + 方法/归属校验 | 4 / 4 | 员工访问 Admin、越权订单、过期 JWT 测试 |
    | 并发安全 | Redis Lua 全有或全无预占，Stream 异步消费，MySQL 条件更新兜底，补偿与对账 | 4 / 4* | 最后库存并发、崩溃窗口、重投、Redis 重启测试 |
    | **合计** |  | **20 / 20*** | `*` 依赖下面的一致性闸门 |
    
    ### 并发安全满分闸门
    
    必须同时满足：
    
    1. Redis Lua 在同一原子操作中完成全部库存检查、扣减、reservation 记录和 `XADD`。
    2. Redis reservation 不代表成功；只有 MySQL 事务确认后才返回/展示成功。
    3. Stream 至少一次投递通过 MySQL 唯一 reservation/idempotency 记录去重。
    4. MySQL 仍执行 `remaining_stock >= requested` 条件更新，不信任 Redis 快照。
    5. 终态失败执行幂等补偿；崩溃重投不会重复扣减或恢复。
    6. 定时对账能发现并修复 Redis、MySQL、movement ledger 的差异。
    
    如果实现成“Redis 扣完立即返回成功，随后尽力异步写 MySQL”，则存在 Redis 数据丢失、消费者失败和双写不一致。该实现不能按 4 / 4 评价，并可能触发库存、实时同步、看板真实数据等 Tier 3 项的级联归零。
    
    ## 5. 认证修改的评分影响
    
    短 JWT + 长 refresh cookie 不直接增加独立分数，但能强化权限项和安全质量。满分实现需满足：
    
    - access JWT 仅存在内存，通过 `Authorization` 发送；
    - refresh token 只在 `HttpOnly + Secure + SameSite + Path` cookie 中；
    - refresh 每次旋转，旧 token 重用会撤销整个 family；
    - JWT 固定算法、校验 issuer/audience/expiry，并支持 key ID 轮换；
    - 统一 Filter 只负责认证并建立 `AuthenticatedActor`，URI/方法/资源归属分别完成授权；
    - 后续应用上下文显式接收 `CurrentActor`，不信任客户端传入 user ID/role。
    
    ## 6. 当前主要扣分风险
    
    1. Redis-first 库存链路比同步 MySQL 更复杂，若没有崩溃窗口和对账测试，设计优势无法转化为分数。
    2. Tier 3 AI 明确不实现，因此不可能获得 Tier 3 满额 10 分。
    3. 后台状态机必须使用条件更新和版本号；仅靠前端隐藏按钮无法证明状态安全。
    4. 完整 AI 对话原始导出是交付铁律，缺失会影响最终评级，即使功能分较高。

On reload, the app calls `/api/v1/auth/refresh`, rotates the cookie, installs the new access JWT, then loads `/api/v1/auth/me`. One request wrapper attaches `Authorization: Bearer ...`, performs a single-flight refresh after an eligible `401`, and retries at most once. Client guards improve UX but are not security boundaries; App/Admin API prefixes and backend role checks are authoritative.
    docs/frontend-design.md:97:SSE is advisory. Initial page load, reconnection, window focus, and a 30-second interval revalidate the menu. A connection badge is unnecessary unless disconnection persists; the UI can quietly fall back to refresh and show a small English notice.

    $ sed -n '1,230p' docs/architecture.md
