    # System architecture
    
    ## 1. Context and deployment
    
    ```mermaid
    flowchart LR
        U[Employee browser\nMobile or desktop] --> SPA[React + TypeScript SPA]
        A[Admin browser\nConsole] --> SPA
        SPA -->|HTTPS JSON + SSE\nBearer access JWT + refresh cookie| API[Spring Boot modular monolith]
        API -->|transactions and reports| DB[(MySQL 8\nsource of truth)]
        API -->|refresh state, cache, rate limits,\nstock reservation, Streams, Pub/Sub| R[(Redis 7)]
        API -->|images in development| FS[(Local upload directory)]
        API -. future adapter .-> LLM[LLM provider or local model]
    ```
    
    The SPA and API are separate projects during development. Vite proxies `/api` and `/media` to Spring Boot so browser security behavior remains close to same-origin production. Public namespaces are deliberately separate: `/api/v1/app/**` for employee capabilities, `/api/v1/admin/**` for Console capabilities, and `/api/v1/auth/**` for token lifecycle. A production build can be served by a reverse proxy or Spring static resources; this does not merge the application layers.
    
    ## 2. Backend module boundaries
    
    ```mermaid
    flowchart TB
        WEB[HTTP/SSE adapters]
        ID[Identity]
        CAT[Catalog]
        MENU[Daily Menu]
        PREF[Preferences]
        ORD[Ordering]
        INV[Inventory]
        REP[Reporting]
        REC[Recommendation port]
        INFRA[MySQL / Redis / media adapters]
    
        WEB --> ID
        WEB --> CAT
        WEB --> MENU
        WEB --> PREF
        WEB --> ORD
        WEB --> REP
        WEB -. feature flag .-> REC
        ORD --> MENU
        ORD --> INV
        ORD --> PREF
        REP --> ORD
        REP --> INV
        REC --> MENU
        REC --> PREF
        REC --> ORD
        ID --> INFRA
        CAT --> INFRA
        MENU --> INFRA
        ORD --> INFRA
        INV --> INFRA
    ```
    
    Modules expose application use cases and read models, not repositories. Cross-module calls use explicit interfaces. The database remains one physical schema. Redis reservations absorb the hot-stock race; MySQL remains the durable authority for orders, confirmed inventory, uniqueness, status history, idempotency completion, and reporting.
    
    ## 3. Critical order flow
    
    ```mermaid
    sequenceDiagram
        participant UI as React checkout
        participant API as Ordering API
        participant REDIS as Redis Lua + Stream
        participant WORKER as Inventory consumer
        participant DB as MySQL
        participant OUT as Outbox publisher
        participant SSE as Connected menu clients
    
        UI->>API: POST /orders + Idempotency-Key
        API->>DB: claim idempotency submission; validate user/slot/catalog/prices
        API->>REDIS: Lua: check all quantities, decrement, save reservation, XADD atomically
        alt Redis has insufficient stock
            REDIS-->>API: rejected items and exact remaining values
            API->>DB: mark submission failed
            API-->>UI: 409 INVENTORY_INSUFFICIENT
        else reserved
            REDIS-->>WORKER: durable reservation event
            WORKER->>DB: idempotent transaction: conditional stock update, order snapshots, movements, history, outbox
            alt durable confirmation completes in request wait budget
                DB-->>API: submission COMPLETED + order ID
                API-->>UI: 201 order and resolved delivery information
            else still processing
                API-->>UI: 202 submission ID; retry/poll with same key
            end
            OUT->>REDIS: publish confirmed stock/dashboard event
            REDIS->>SSE: fan out versioned stock event
            SSE-->>UI: update TanStack Query cache
        end
    ```
    
    All stock keys for a delivery date use one Redis Cluster hash tag so a Lua script can check every requested dish, decrement them, persist a reservation record, and append its Redis Stream event atomically. A consumer group performs MySQL conditional updates in stable item-ID order. It creates the order only after every update succeeds, and uses the reservation/idempotency ID as a unique deduplication key.
    
    The asynchronous boundary is internal: **a Redis reservation alone is never an accepted order**. The API waits briefly for durable confirmation; on timeout it returns `202 Processing`, and the frontend polls/retries the submission with the same idempotency key. Only a MySQL-confirmed order returns `201` and appears as `Pending`.
    
    Failure handling is explicit:
    
    - consumer crash before commit leaves the Stream entry pending for redelivery;
    - crash after commit but before acknowledgement is harmless because the MySQL deduplication key returns the existing order;
    - a terminal MySQL conflict triggers an idempotent Redis compensation and a failed submission;
    - Redis loss before MySQL confirmation cannot produce a false success;
    - Redis loss after MySQL confirmation is repaired from MySQL by reconciliation, while the existing order remains durable;
    - scheduled reconciliation compares Redis values, `daily_menu_item`, and the movement ledger, alerts on drift, and rebuilds only from MySQL-confirmed state.
    
    Redis uses AOF and a `noeviction` policy for reservation/Stream keys. MySQL still uses `UPDATE ... WHERE remaining_stock >= ?` as the last invariant, so Redis split-brain or stale initialization fails safely rather than overselling.
    
    ## 4. Consistency model
    
    | Data | Authority | Cache/event behavior | Consistency guarantee |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | Users, preferences, dishes, orders | MySQL | Redis entries invalidated after committed outbox event | DB read is authoritative |
    | Menu metadata | MySQL | Redis cache-aside, versioned keys, 5-minute TTL; admin mutations invalidate | Bounded staleness for browse; checkout revalidates |
    | Inventory reservation | Redis Lua + Stream | Atomic all-item reservation; reservation is not final success | Serialized admission, recoverable processing |
    | Confirmed inventory | MySQL `remaining_stock` + ledger | Post-confirmation version/value pushed over SSE; periodic reconciliation | Durable invariant and reporting authority |
    | Refresh token family | Redis hashed rotating token state | TTL, reuse detection, logout revocation | Long-lived login without exposing refresh token to JavaScript |
    | Dashboard | MySQL aggregate queries | 15-second cache; order/menu events invalidate | Real order source, near-real-time display |
    | Idempotency | MySQL unique submission record | Reservation ID links Redis and consumer | Durable duplicate prevention and processing lookup |
    
    Versioned confirmed-stock events let clients and cache writers ignore out-of-order delivery. If post-commit publication fails, the MySQL outbox retains the event for retry. Menu reads can bypass a failed metadata cache. New orders fail closed when reservation Redis is unavailable; existing short-lived access JWTs remain usable until expiration, while refresh requires Redis recovery.
    
    ## 5. Security and trust boundaries
    
    - Spring Security owns authentication and authorization. Passwords are BCrypt hashes and never logged.
    - Login returns a 10–15 minute signed access JWT for in-memory client use and sets a rotating 7–30 day opaque refresh token cookie. The refresh cookie is `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Strict` (or `Lax` if deployment navigation requires it), path-scoped to `/api/v1/auth`, and `Secure` outside local development. Only a token hash/family record is stored in Redis.
    - `JwtAuthenticationFilter` extends `OncePerRequestFilter`, validates signature/issuer/audience/expiry, and creates one immutable `AuthenticatedActor(userId, role)` in Spring's `SecurityContext`. Controllers obtain it with `@AuthenticationPrincipal`; application use cases receive an explicit `CurrentActor` rather than reading a custom global `ThreadLocal`.
    - One `SecurityFilterChain` applies namespace rules: `/api/v1/app/**` requires `EMPLOYEE`, `/api/v1/admin/**` requires `ADMIN`, and token endpoints use explicit public/authenticated rules. Method-level ownership and transition checks remain mandatory, so URI checks are not the only protection.
    - Refresh rotates on every use. Reuse of an old token revokes the whole family. Logout revokes the family; access JWTs expire naturally or can be denied by a short TTL revocation key for high-risk logout.
    - Request DTOs use Jakarta Bean Validation and explicit maximum lengths. JPA/parameterized SQL prevents SQL injection; user sort fields map through an allowlist.
    - Login and order endpoints use Redis-backed token-bucket limits by IP and authenticated user. A Redis limiter failure uses conservative local limits and emits an alert.
    - Uploads allow JPEG/PNG/WebP, verify decoded MIME type, cap size and dimensions, generate server filenames, and store only relative URLs. Executable/SVG input is rejected.
    - Error responses use stable English codes and safe messages; stack traces and SQL details never cross the API boundary.
    
    ## 6. Reliability and operations
    
    - Flyway owns schema evolution; no `ddl-auto=update`. Startup validates the schema.
    - Liveness reports process state. Readiness checks MySQL and reservation Redis; short-lived access-token verification itself remains local.
    - JSON logs include correlation ID, route, latency, status, and non-sensitive user/order identifiers.
    - Metrics cover request latency, cache hit ratio, rate-limit rejection, order outcomes, inventory conflicts, SSE connections, outbox lag, and dashboard query time.
    - Graceful shutdown stops accepting traffic, drains requests, and closes SSE connections with a reconnect hint.
    - Daily reconciliation compares order-item quantities against inventory movements; an `inventory_movement` ledger makes anomalies auditable.
    - Database backups and restore tests are deployment concerns; local development keeps uploaded media under a documented persistent path.
    
    ## 7. AI recommendation extension
    
    The `recommendation` module defines `RecommendationProvider.recommend(RecommendationContext)` and does not depend on a specific LLM SDK. Context contains the English prompt, sanitized preferences, available/non-sold-out menu, allergen exclusions, and dish IDs ordered in the previous seven days.
    
    Safety and correctness remain outside the model:
    
    1. The application removes allergen and sold-out candidates before sending context.
    2. The provider returns structured dish IDs plus reasons, never authoritative dish data.
    3. The application validates returned IDs again and hydrates current cards from the menu module.
    4. `/api/v1/app/recommendations/stream` uses SSE events (`status`, `recommendation`, `complete`, `error`) so the UI can progressively render cards.
    5. A feature flag hides the entry when no provider/API key is configured. Provider secrets live only in environment variables.
    
    This seam supports Spring AI/OpenAI-compatible APIs or a local model later without changing menu/cart contracts.
    
    | `menu` | date publication, offered dishes, initial stock, browse queries | decrement correctness |
    | `preference` | allergen/cuisine/taste/spice/budget settings | LLM logic |
    | `ordering` | cutoff normalization, quote, submission/idempotency, active-order rule, snapshots, audited state machine | cache implementation |
    | `inventory` | Redis reservation, idempotent MySQL confirmation/restore, movement ledger, compensation/reconciliation | cart quantities before checkout |
    | `reporting` | read-only aggregates and DTOs | duplicated reporting tables initially |
    | `recommendation` | context assembly/provider port/policy post-filter | authoritative menu or allergy data |
    
    ## 4. Transaction design
    
    ### Place order
    
    The request and asynchronous consumer form one recoverable workflow:
    
    1. Validate `Idempotency-Key`, canonicalize the request, and claim `(user_id, key)` in MySQL with request hash and `PROCESSING` state.
    2. Normalize date/slot, check the active-order/submission rule, validate current catalog/options, and calculate `BigDecimal` totals.
    3. Call one date-scoped Redis Lua script. It verifies all quantities, decrements all-or-none, stores the reservation, and `XADD`s a reservation event atomically.
    4. A Redis Stream consumer group reads the event and opens a MySQL transaction. It rechecks the idempotency/reservation state, conditionally updates confirmed stock in stable item order, inserts the order/snapshots/movements/initial status history/outbox rows, and completes the idempotency record.
    5. After commit it acknowledges the Stream entry and marks the Redis reservation confirmed. Redelivery observes the completed unique reservation and does not decrement again.
    6. The HTTP request waits within a configured budget. It returns `201` if completed, `202` with a submission/status URL if still processing, `409` for a deterministic conflict, or the previously completed order for a same-key retry.
    
    If MySQL rejects a reservation because reconciliation found drift or a competing invariant, the consumer performs an idempotent Lua compensation and marks the submission `FAILED`. A pending-entry reaper retries transient failures; a reservation-expiry worker compensates only when MySQL proves the submission never completed. User-visible success is therefore never based only on Redis.
    
    ### Order state machine and cancellation
    
    All transitions use `UPDATE ... WHERE id = ? AND status = ? AND version = ?`, append `order_status_history`, and enqueue an outbox event in the same MySQL transaction.
    
    | Actor | From | To | Inventory effect |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | Employee | `PENDING` | `CANCELLED` | Restore exactly once |
    | Admin | `PENDING` | `CONFIRMED` | None |
    | Admin | `PENDING` | `CANCELLED` | Restore exactly once |
    | Admin | `CONFIRMED` | `COMPLETED` | None |
    | Admin | `CONFIRMED` | `CANCELLED` | Restore exactly once |
    
    `COMPLETED` and `CANCELLED` are terminal. Restoration is committed in MySQL first and emitted through the outbox to Redis; a temporarily stale Redis value is conservative (too low), and versioned reconciliation repairs it. The unique inventory movement prevents a repeated transition from restoring twice. No operator queue is required.
    
    ## 5. Authentication and actor context
    
    - Access JWT lifetime defaults to 15 minutes. It contains subject/user ID, role, issuer, audience, issued-at, expiry, and token ID; no profile or preference data.
    - The SPA stores access JWT only in memory and sends `Authorization: Bearer ...`. It never stores either token in `localStorage`.
    - Refresh is an opaque 256-bit value in a path-scoped `HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `SameSite` cookie. Redis stores only an HMAC/hash, user ID, family ID, generation, expiry, and revoked flag.
    - `/api/v1/auth/refresh` rotates token and cookie in one atomic Redis Lua operation. Old-token reuse revokes the family and emits a security event.
    - `JwtAuthenticationFilter` builds `AuthenticatedActor`; `SecurityFilterChain` applies App/Admin prefixes. `@PreAuthorize` and domain checks enforce ownership and transition rights.
    - Key IDs support signing-key rotation. Clock skew is bounded; issuer/audience/algorithm are fixed, never accepted from untrusted headers.
    
    ## 6. Pricing and validation
    
    - MySQL uses `DECIMAL(10,2)`; Java uses `BigDecimal` with scale 2 and `RoundingMode.UNNECESSARY` for persisted prices.
    - Option values contribute a fixed `price_delta`. An item unit price is dish base price plus selected deltas; subtotal is unit price × quantity.
    - Required groups must have exactly one selection. Single-select groups accept at most one. Multi-select groups de-duplicate values.
    - All selected values must belong to the chosen dish and be active.
    - Order snapshots preserve dish/option names and prices even after catalog edits.
    - Search term ≤ 50, email/address ≤ 200, names/descriptions/options have limits reflected in DTO and DDL.
    
    ## 7. Cutoff policy
    
    `MealSlotResolver` is a pure, clock-driven component:
    
    ```text
    requested future slot                 -> unchanged
    today Lunch before 10:00              -> today Lunch
    today Lunch at/after 10:00 before 15:00 -> today Dinner
    today Lunch/Dinner at/after 15:00      -> tomorrow Lunch
    today Dinner before 15:00             -> today Dinner
    past date                              -> validation error
    ```
    
    Cutoff is exclusive: exactly `10:00:00` closes Lunch and exactly `15:00:00` closes Dinner. The quote endpoint returns the resolved slot and warnings before `Place Order`; the order endpoint repeats resolution to prevent time-of-check/time-of-use errors.
    
    ## 8. Cache, inventory stream, and real-time behavior
    
    ### Menu cache
    
    - Key: `webox:menu:v{schemaVersion}:{date}:{publicationVersion}`.
    - Value: normalized English dish/menu metadata. Query-specific filtering and pagination occur over the bounded daily set to avoid a combinatorial key space.
    - TTL: five minutes plus ±10% jitter. Admin dish/menu changes bump the date publication version after commit.
    - Inventory values in responses are merged from current menu-item rows or exact versioned stock cache entries; checkout always uses MySQL.
    - A short Redis `SET NX PX` rebuild lease prevents stampedes. Callers that miss the lease read MySQL instead of waiting indefinitely.
    
    ### Inventory reservation and reconciliation
    
    - Keys use `{inventory:YYYY-MM-DD}` hash tags so every item in an order is in one Redis Cluster slot.
    - Lua records reservation ID and before/after versions and appends a Stream entry in the same command. Reservation commands are idempotent.
    - Consumer concurrency is bounded; each message has a unique MySQL reservation/idempotency key. `XAUTOCLAIM` recovers abandoned pending entries.
    - Redis uses persistence and `noeviction` for critical keys. A daily bootstrap loads from MySQL only when no unprocessed reservation exists.
    - Continuous reconciliation compares MySQL confirmed stock plus movements with Redis confirmed/reserved quantities. Drift pages an operator, blocks the affected date from new reservations, and repairs from MySQL plus still-valid reservations.
    
    ### Events and SSE
    
    - The transactional outbox publisher claims rows using `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, publishes versioned events, and records publication time.
    - Redis channel is an internal fan-out transport; browser clients connect to `/api/v1/app/menu/stock-stream?date=...`.
    - SSE sends `stock.changed`, heartbeat, and `resync.required`. It never exposes internal entity payloads.
    - On initial connect/reconnect the UI fetches a fresh menu page; it also revalidates on window focus and every 30 seconds.
    
    ### Rate limits
    
    Redis Lua performs atomic token-bucket updates. Proposed defaults are 10 login attempts/10 minutes/IP, 10 order attempts/minute/user, and 120 general API requests/minute/user. Values are configuration, not hard-coded policy. `429` includes `Retry-After`.
    
    ## 9. Dashboard queries
    
    The reporting repository uses explicit, indexed SQL and maps directly to immutable DTOs:
    
    - overview groups by status for a delivery date; revenue sums non-cancelled order totals;
    - Top 10 sums item quantities for non-cancelled orders and uses item snapshot names;
    - meal split counts non-cancelled orders by `meal_slot`;
    - trend groups delivery date for the inclusive last seven dates;
    - low stock reads today's published menu where remaining stock ≤ 3.
    
    Results cache for 15 seconds and are invalidated by order placed/cancelled/status-changed and menu-stock events. The endpoint also supports `refresh=true` for the Console refresh button, restricted and rate-limited.
    
    ## 10. Error contract and status codes
    
    Spring `ProblemDetail` is extended with `code`, `correlationId`, `fieldErrors`, and optional `details`. Messages are English.
    
    | Condition | HTTP | Code |
    |---|---:|---|
    | Invalid input | 400 | `VALIDATION_FAILED` |
    | Missing/invalid/expired access JWT | 401 | `AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED` |
    | Wrong role/ownership | 403 | `ACCESS_DENIED` |
    | Missing resource | 404 | `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND` |
    | Active order exists | 409 | `ACTIVE_ORDER_EXISTS` |
    | Idempotency conflict/in progress | 409 | `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED` / `REQUEST_IN_PROGRESS` |
    | Inventory conflict | 409 | `INVENTORY_INSUFFICIENT` |
    | Invalid order transition/version | 409 | `INVALID_ORDER_TRANSITION` / `OPTIMISTIC_LOCK_CONFLICT` |
    | Business cutoff/menu mismatch | 422 | `ORDER_SLOT_ADJUSTED` or `MENU_ITEM_UNAVAILABLE` |
    | Rate limited | 429 | `RATE_LIMITED` |
    
    The quote endpoint normally handles slot adjustment as a successful response with a warning. `ORDER_SLOT_ADJUSTED` is reserved for a placement request whose resolved slot changed after the quote and requires explicit user review.
    
    ## 11. Test strategy
    
    - **Unit:** cutoff boundaries, five-serving rule, price calculations, required options, preference ranking, allergen matching.
    - **Application:** use cases with fake clock and ports; authorization and ownership paths.
    - **Integration:** Testcontainers MySQL/Redis; Flyway, JWT/refresh rotation and reuse, URI roles, repository SQL, Stream recovery, cache invalidation, outbox, idempotency, and every state transition.
    - **Concurrency:** 20+ threads reserve the last N units; assert MySQL-confirmed successful quantity equals N, stock is zero, Redis/MySQL reconcile, movement ledger balances, and no duplicate active order exists.
    - **API:** MockMvc/OpenAPI-compatible request/response and English errors.
    - **Failure:** crash before/after MySQL commit and Stream acknowledgement, duplicate delivery, Redis restart/loss, compensation retry, outbox retry, deadlock retry, refresh-token reuse, and signing-key rotation.
    
    # Proposed API contract
    
    JSON uses `camelCase`, date-only values use ISO `YYYY-MM-DD`, instants use ISO-8601 with an offset, and money is a two-decimal string such as `"22.50"`. All user-facing messages are English.
    
    | Namespace | Audience | Authorization |
    |---|---|---|
    | `/api/v1/auth/**` | token lifecycle | Explicit public/authenticated rule per endpoint |
    | `/api/v1/app/**` | employee application | `Bearer` JWT with `EMPLOYEE` role |
    | `/api/v1/admin/**` | Console | `Bearer` JWT with `ADMIN` role |
    
    ## 1. Common behavior
    
    - API calls use `Authorization: Bearer <short-lived-access-jwt>`.
    - The rotating refresh token is an opaque value in a path-scoped `HttpOnly` cookie and is sent only to `/api/v1/auth/refresh` or logout. Access tokens remain in browser memory.
    - List endpoints return `{items, page, size, totalItems, totalPages}`. Default/max sizes are 20/100.
    - State-changing requests accept `X-Correlation-Id`; the server creates one if absent.
    - Order placement requires `Idempotency-Key: <UUID>`.
    - Errors use `application/problem+json`:
    
    ```json
    {
      "type": "https://webox.local/problems/inventory-insufficient",
      "title": "Inventory is insufficient",
      "status": 409,
      "detail": "Some dishes no longer have the requested quantity.",
      "code": "INVENTORY_INSUFFICIENT",
      "correlationId": "01J...",
      "details": {
        "items": [{"dishId": 9, "name": "Classic Beef Burger", "requested": 2, "remaining": 1}]
      }
    }
    ```
    
    ## 2. Authentication and current user
    
    | Method | Path | Access | Purpose |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | POST | `/api/v1/auth/register` | public | Register employee, return access JWT, set refresh cookie |
    | POST | `/api/v1/auth/login` | public | Return access JWT, set refresh cookie |
    | POST | `/api/v1/auth/refresh` | refresh cookie | Rotate refresh family and return access JWT |
    | POST | `/api/v1/auth/logout` | refresh cookie/access JWT | Revoke refresh family and clear cookie |
    | GET | `/api/v1/auth/me` | access JWT | Current ID, email, role, display name |
    
    Registration accepts `email`, `password`, and `displayName`; password is 8–72 characters with at least one ASCII letter and digit. Login errors intentionally use a generic `Invalid email or password.` response.
    
    ## 3. Menu, dishes, and stock
    
    | Method | Path | Access | Purpose |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | GET | `/api/v1/app/menu?date=&q=&categories=&page=&size=&recommended=` | employee | Published daily menu with stock and preference metadata |
    | GET | `/api/v1/app/menu/dishes/{dishId}?date=` | employee | Dish details and active customization options |
    | GET | `/api/v1/app/menu/stock-stream?date=` | employee | SSE stock updates |
    
    Menu items include `menuItemId`, `dish`, `basePrice`, `remainingStock`, `stockVersion`, `lowStock`, `soldOut`, and optional `preferenceMatches`. `q` is at most 50 characters; categories are allowlisted enums.
    
    SSE event example:
    
    ```text
    event: stock.changed
    id: 18442
    data: {"date":"2026-10-24","menuItemId":9,"remainingStock":2,"stockVersion":7}
    ```
    
    ## 4. Preferences and addresses
    
    | Method | Path | Access | Purpose |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | GET | `/api/v1/app/preferences` | employee | Get dietary preferences |
    | PUT | `/api/v1/app/preferences` | employee | Replace validated preference set |
    | GET | `/api/v1/app/addresses` | employee | List saved addresses |
    | POST | `/api/v1/app/addresses` | employee | Add address |
    | PATCH | `/api/v1/app/addresses/{id}` | owner | Edit/default address |
    | DELETE | `/api/v1/app/addresses/{id}` | owner | Soft-delete address |
    
    Preference payload contains `allergens`, `preferredCategories`, `spiceLevel`, `tasteIntensity`, `budgetMin`, and `budgetMax`. Budget values are nullable decimal strings and min must not exceed max.
    
    ## 5. Quote and orders
    
    | Method | Path | Access | Purpose |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | POST | `/api/v1/app/orders/quote` | employee | Reprice and validate cart; resolve slot |
    | POST | `/api/v1/app/orders` | employee | Start idempotent reservation/order submission |
    | GET | `/api/v1/app/order-submissions/{submissionId}` | owner | Poll asynchronous durable confirmation |
    | GET | `/api/v1/app/orders?page=&size=&status=` | employee | Own order history |
    | GET | `/api/v1/app/orders/{orderId}` | owner | Order details and snapshots |
    | POST | `/api/v1/app/orders/{orderId}/cancel` | owner | Cancel a Pending order and restore stock |
    
    Quote/place request shape:
    
    ```json
    {
      "deliveryDate": "2026-10-24",
      "mealSlot": "LUNCH",
      "addressId": 12,
      "newAddress": null,
      "acceptedResolvedSlot": null,
      "items": [
        {"dishId": 4, "quantity": 2, "optionValueIds": [8, 11]}
      ]
    }
    ```
    
    The quote response contains authoritative line prices, `total`, `resolvedDeliveryDate`, `resolvedMealSlot`, `slotAdjusted`, warnings (`BUDGET_EXCEEDED`, etc.), unavailable items, and optional `existingOrder`. When a new address is supplied, it is validated and saved only as part of successful placement if `saveAddress=true`.
    
    `POST /api/v1/app/orders` returns `201` when MySQL confirmation finishes in the wait budget, `202` with `submissionId`, `statusUrl`, and `Retry-After` while still processing, and `200` when replaying the completed same request/key. A reused key with a different request returns `409`. The UI never displays order success for `202`; it polls or safely retries with the same key. An active-order conflict includes its order ID so the UI can offer `View Existing Order`.
    
    ## 6. Console catalog and menus
    
    All endpoints require `ADMIN`.
    
    | Method | Path | Purpose |
    |---|---|---|
    | GET | `/api/v1/admin/dishes?q=&category=&published=&page=&size=` | Search/filter all dishes |
    | POST | `/api/v1/admin/dishes` | Create English dish and option definitions |
    | GET | `/api/v1/admin/dishes/{id}` | Edit model |
    | PUT | `/api/v1/admin/dishes/{id}` | Replace editable data with optimistic version |
    | PATCH | `/api/v1/admin/dishes/{id}/publication` | Publish/unpublish |
    | POST | `/api/v1/admin/media/images` | Validated image upload; returns relative media URL |
    | GET | `/api/v1/admin/menus/{date}` | Dated menu editor model |
    | PUT | `/api/v1/admin/menus/{date}` | Create/update offered dishes and initial/current stock |
    | POST | `/api/v1/admin/menus/{date}/publish` | Publish menu |
    
    Dish writes contain `version`; stale edits return `409 OPTIMISTIC_LOCK_CONFLICT`. Menu stock cannot be reduced below already sold net quantity. Removing an offered item with order references is disallowed; it can be hidden from new browsing instead.
    
    ## 7. Admin order processing
    
    All endpoints require `ADMIN`. Operator actions are synchronous commands; no operator queue is used.
    
    | Method | Path | Purpose |
    |---|---|---|
    | GET | `/api/v1/admin/orders?q=&date=&mealSlot=&status=&page=&size=` | Search/filter all orders |
    | GET | `/api/v1/admin/orders/{orderId}` | Order details plus status history |
    | POST | `/api/v1/admin/orders/{orderId}/transitions` | Apply a permitted conditional transition |
    
    Transition payload is `{targetStatus, expectedVersion, reason}`. Allowed transitions are `PENDING → CONFIRMED`, `PENDING → CANCELLED`, `CONFIRMED → COMPLETED`, and `CONFIRMED → CANCELLED`. Terminal states reject changes with `409 INVALID_ORDER_TRANSITION`; a stale version returns `409 OPTIMISTIC_LOCK_CONFLICT`. Every successful transition records admin actor, from/to status, reason, and timestamp.
    
    ## 8. Console dashboard
    
    | Method | Path | Access | Purpose |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | GET | `/api/v1/admin/dashboard?date=&refresh=` | admin | One consistent dashboard snapshot |
    
    Response shape:
    
    ```json
    {
      "asOf": "2026-10-24T11:05:00+08:00",
      "date": "2026-10-24",
      "overview": {"totalOrders": 42, "revenue": "1188.50", "completed": 10, "pending": 20, "confirmed": 9, "cancelled": 3},
      "topDishes": [{"dishId": 1, "name": "Kung Pao Chicken", "quantity": 21}],
      "mealDistribution": [{"mealSlot": "LUNCH", "orders": 36}, {"mealSlot": "DINNER", "orders": 3}],
      "trend": [{"date": "2026-10-18", "orders": 31, "revenue": "850.00"}],
      "lowStock": [{"dishId": 9, "name": "Classic Beef Burger", "remainingStock": 2}]
    }
    ```
    
    ## 9. Reserved AI extension
    
    `POST /api/v1/app/recommendations/stream` is reserved behind `features.aiRecommendation`. It accepts `{prompt, date, mealSlot}` and returns SSE `status`, `recommendation`, `complete`, or `error` events. Recommendation events contain validated current dish cards and an English reason. When disabled the endpoint returns `404 FEATURE_NOT_AVAILABLE`, avoiding a non-functional UI control.
    
      stock_version BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
      created_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      updated_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3) ON UPDATE CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      UNIQUE KEY uk_daily_menu_dish (daily_menu_id, dish_id),
      KEY idx_menu_item_stock (daily_menu_id, remaining_stock, visible),
      CONSTRAINT fk_menu_item_menu FOREIGN KEY (daily_menu_id) REFERENCES daily_menu (id),
      CONSTRAINT fk_menu_item_dish FOREIGN KEY (dish_id) REFERENCES dish (id),
      CONSTRAINT ck_menu_item_stock CHECK (remaining_stock <= initial_stock)
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE customer_order (
      id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
      order_number VARCHAR(32) NOT NULL,
      user_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      delivery_date DATE NOT NULL,
      meal_slot VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
      delivery_address VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL,
      status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
      total_amount DECIMAL(10,2) NOT NULL,
      active_slot_guard TINYINT AS (CASE WHEN status IN ('PENDING', 'CONFIRMED') THEN 1 ELSE NULL END) STORED,
      version BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
      created_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      updated_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3) ON UPDATE CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      cancelled_at DATETIME(3) NULL,
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      UNIQUE KEY uk_order_number (order_number),
      UNIQUE KEY uk_active_order_slot (user_id, delivery_date, meal_slot, active_slot_guard),
      KEY idx_order_user_created (user_id, created_at DESC),
      KEY idx_order_dashboard (delivery_date, status, meal_slot),
      CONSTRAINT fk_order_user FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES app_user (id),
      CONSTRAINT ck_order_slot CHECK (meal_slot IN ('LUNCH', 'DINNER')),
      CONSTRAINT ck_order_status CHECK (status IN ('PENDING', 'CONFIRMED', 'COMPLETED', 'CANCELLED')),
      CONSTRAINT ck_order_total CHECK (total_amount >= 0)
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE order_item (
      id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
      order_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      daily_menu_item_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      dish_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      dish_name_snapshot VARCHAR(120) NOT NULL,
      base_price_snapshot DECIMAL(10,2) NOT NULL,
      unit_price DECIMAL(10,2) NOT NULL,
      quantity SMALLINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      subtotal DECIMAL(10,2) NOT NULL,
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      KEY idx_order_item_order (order_id),
      KEY idx_order_item_dish_reporting (dish_id, order_id),
      CONSTRAINT fk_order_item_order FOREIGN KEY (order_id) REFERENCES customer_order (id),
      CONSTRAINT fk_order_item_menu_item FOREIGN KEY (daily_menu_item_id) REFERENCES daily_menu_item (id),
      CONSTRAINT fk_order_item_dish FOREIGN KEY (dish_id) REFERENCES dish (id),
      CONSTRAINT ck_order_item_quantity CHECK (quantity BETWEEN 1 AND 5),
      CONSTRAINT ck_order_item_money CHECK (base_price_snapshot >= 0 AND unit_price >= 0 AND subtotal >= 0)
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE order_status_history (
      id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
      order_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      from_status VARCHAR(20) NULL,
      to_status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
      actor_user_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      actor_role VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
      reason VARCHAR(500) NULL,
      created_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      KEY idx_order_status_history_order (order_id, created_at),
      CONSTRAINT fk_order_status_history_order FOREIGN KEY (order_id) REFERENCES customer_order (id),
      CONSTRAINT fk_order_status_history_actor FOREIGN KEY (actor_user_id) REFERENCES app_user (id),
      CONSTRAINT ck_order_status_history_from CHECK (from_status IS NULL OR from_status IN ('PENDING', 'CONFIRMED', 'COMPLETED', 'CANCELLED')),
      CONSTRAINT ck_order_status_history_to CHECK (to_status IN ('PENDING', 'CONFIRMED', 'COMPLETED', 'CANCELLED')),
      CONSTRAINT ck_order_status_history_role CHECK (actor_role IN ('EMPLOYEE', 'ADMIN', 'SYSTEM'))
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE order_item_option (
      id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
      order_item_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      option_group_name_snapshot VARCHAR(80) NOT NULL,
      option_value_name_snapshot VARCHAR(80) NOT NULL,
      price_delta_snapshot DECIMAL(10,2) NOT NULL,
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      KEY idx_order_item_option_item (order_item_id),
      CONSTRAINT fk_order_option_item FOREIGN KEY (order_item_id) REFERENCES order_item (id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
      CONSTRAINT ck_order_option_price CHECK (price_delta_snapshot >= 0)
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE idempotency_record (
      id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
      user_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      idempotency_key CHAR(36) NOT NULL,
      request_hash CHAR(64) NOT NULL,
      reservation_id CHAR(36) NULL,
      state VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
      order_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NULL,
      response_status SMALLINT UNSIGNED NULL,
      failure_code VARCHAR(100) NULL,
      created_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      completed_at DATETIME(3) NULL,
      expires_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL,
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      UNIQUE KEY uk_idempotency_user_key (user_id, idempotency_key),
      UNIQUE KEY uk_idempotency_reservation (reservation_id),
      KEY idx_idempotency_expiry (expires_at),
      CONSTRAINT fk_idempotency_user FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES app_user (id),
      CONSTRAINT fk_idempotency_order FOREIGN KEY (order_id) REFERENCES customer_order (id),
      CONSTRAINT ck_idempotency_state CHECK (state IN ('PROCESSING', 'COMPLETED', 'FAILED'))
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE inventory_movement (
      id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
      daily_menu_item_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      order_id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      movement_type VARCHAR(30) NOT NULL,
      quantity_delta INT NOT NULL,
      stock_after INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
      created_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      UNIQUE KEY uk_inventory_order_type_item (order_id, movement_type, daily_menu_item_id),
      KEY idx_inventory_menu_time (daily_menu_item_id, created_at),
      CONSTRAINT fk_inventory_menu_item FOREIGN KEY (daily_menu_item_id) REFERENCES daily_menu_item (id),
      CONSTRAINT fk_inventory_order FOREIGN KEY (order_id) REFERENCES customer_order (id),
      CONSTRAINT ck_inventory_type CHECK (movement_type IN ('ORDER_DEDUCT', 'CANCEL_RESTORE')),
      CONSTRAINT ck_inventory_delta CHECK (quantity_delta <> 0)
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;
    
    CREATE TABLE outbox_event (
      id BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
      aggregate_type VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
      aggregate_id VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
      event_type VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
      payload JSON NOT NULL,
      occurred_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      published_at DATETIME(3) NULL,
      attempt_count INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
      next_attempt_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3),
      PRIMARY KEY (id),
      KEY idx_outbox_unpublished (published_at, next_attempt_at, id)
    ) ENGINE=InnoDB;

    # Verification Before Completion
    
    ## Overview
    
    **Core principle:** Evidence before claims, always.
    
    **Violating the letter of this rule is violating the spirit of this rule.**
    
    ## The Iron Law
    
    ```
    NO COMPLETION CLAIMS WITHOUT FRESH VERIFICATION EVIDENCE
    ```
    
    If you haven't run the verification command in this message, you cannot claim it passes.
    
    ## The Gate Function
    
    ```
    BEFORE claiming any status or expressing satisfaction:
    
    1. IDENTIFY: What command proves this claim?
    2. RUN: Execute the FULL command (fresh, complete)
    3. READ: Full output, check exit code, count failures
    4. VERIFY: Does output confirm the claim?
       - If NO: State actual status with evidence
       - If YES: State claim WITH evidence
    5. ONLY THEN: Make the claim
    
    Skip any step = lying, not verifying
    ```
    
    ## Common Failures
    
    | Claim | Requires | Not Sufficient |
    |-------|----------|----------------|
    | Tests pass | Test command output: 0 failures | Previous run, "should pass" |
    | Linter clean | Linter output: 0 errors | Partial check, extrapolation |
    | Build succeeds | Build command: exit 0 | Linter passing, logs look good |
    | Bug fixed | Test original symptom: passes | Code changed, assumed fixed |
    | Regression test works | Red-green cycle verified | Test passes once |
    | Agent completed | VCS diff shows changes | Agent reports "success" |
    | Requirements met | Line-by-line checklist | Tests passing |
    
    ## Red Flags - STOP
    
    - Using "should", "probably", "seems to"
    - Expressing satisfaction before verification ("Great!", "Perfect!", "Done!", etc.)
    - About to commit/push/PR without verification
    - Trusting agent success reports
    - Relying on partial verification
    - Thinking "just this once"
    - Tired and wanting work over
    - **ANY wording implying success without having run verification**
    
    ## Rationalization Prevention
    
    | Excuse | Reality |
    |--------|---------|
    | "Should work now" | RUN the verification |
    | "I'm confident" | Confidence ≠ evidence |
    | "Just this once" | No exceptions |
    | "Linter passed" | Linter ≠ compiler |
    | "Agent said success" | Verify independently |
    | "I'm tired" | Exhaustion ≠ excuse |
    | "Partial check is enough" | Partial proves nothing |
    | "Different words so rule doesn't apply" | Spirit over letter |
    
    ## Key Patterns
    
    **Tests:**
    ```
    ✅ [Run test command] [See: 34/34 pass] "All tests pass"
    ❌ "Should pass now" / "Looks correct"
    ```
    
    **Regression tests (TDD Red-Green):**
    ```
    ✅ Write → Run (pass) → Revert fix → Run (MUST FAIL) → Restore → Run (pass)
    ❌ "I've written a regression test" (without red-green verification)
    ```
    
    **Build:**
    ```
    ✅ [Run build] [See: exit 0] "Build passes"
    ❌ "Linter passed" (linter doesn't check compilation)
    ```
    
    **Requirements:**
    ```
    ✅ Re-read plan → Create checklist → Verify each → Report gaps or completion
    ❌ "Tests pass, phase complete"
    ```
    
    **Agent delegation:**
    ```
    ✅ Agent reports success → Check VCS diff → Verify changes → Report actual state
    ❌ Trust agent report
    ```
    
    ## When To Apply
    
    **ALWAYS before:**
    - ANY variation of success/completion claims
    - ANY expression of satisfaction
    - ANY positive statement about work state
    - Committing, PR creation, task completion
    - Moving to next task
    - Delegating to agents
    
    **Rule applies to:**
    - Exact phrases
    - Paraphrases and synonyms
    - Implications of success
    - ANY communication suggesting completion/correctness
    ✓ • 0ms

    name: using-superpowers
    description: Use when starting any conversation - establishes how to find and use skills, requiring skill invocation before ANY response including clarifying questions
    ---
    
    <SUBAGENT-STOP>
    If you were dispatched as a subagent to execute a specific task, ignore this skill.
    </SUBAGENT-STOP>
    
    <EXTREMELY-IMPORTANT>
    If you think there is even a 1% chance a skill might apply to what you are doing, you ABSOLUTELY MUST invoke the skill.
    
    IF A SKILL APPLIES TO YOUR TASK, YOU DO NOT HAVE A CHOICE. YOU MUST USE IT.
    
    This is not negotiable. You cannot rationalize your way out of this.
    </EXTREMELY-IMPORTANT>
    
    ## The Rule
    
    **Invoke relevant or requested skills BEFORE any response or action** — including clarifying questions, exploring the codebase, or checking files. If it turns out wrong for the situation, you don't have to use it.
    
    **Before entering plan mode:** if you haven't already brainstormed, invoke the brainstorming skill first.
    
    Then announce "Using [skill] to [purpose]" and follow the skill exactly. If it has a checklist, create a todo per item.
    
    ## Skill Priority
    
    When multiple skills apply, process skills come first — they set the approach, then implementation skills (frontend-design, etc.) carry it out. Brainstorming and systematic-debugging are Superpowers' most common process skills, but the rule holds for any of them.
    
    - "Let's build X" → superpowers:brainstorming first, then implementation skills.
    - "Fix this bug" → superpowers:systematic-debugging first, then domain skills.
    
    ## Red Flags
    
    These thoughts mean STOP—you're rationalizing:
    
    | Thought | Reality |
    |---------|---------|
    | "This is just a simple question" | Questions are tasks. Check for skills. |
    | "I need more context first" | Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions. |
    | "Let me explore the codebase first" | Skills tell you HOW to explore. Check first. |
    | "I can check git/files quickly" | Files lack conversation context. Check for skills. |
    | "Let me gather information first" | Skills tell you HOW to gather information. |
    | "This doesn't need a formal skill" | If a skill exists, use it. |
    | "I remember this skill" | Skills evolve. Read current version. |
    | "This doesn't count as a task" | Action = task. Check for skills. |
    | "The skill is overkill" | Simple things become complex. Use it. |
    | "I'll just do this one thing first" | Check BEFORE doing anything. |
    | "This feels productive" | Undisciplined action wastes time. Skills prevent this. |
    | "I know what that means" | Knowing the concept ≠ using the skill. Invoke it. |
    
    ## Platform Adaptation
    
    If your harness appears here, read its reference file for special instructions:
    
    - Codex: `references/codex-tools.md`
    - Pi: `references/pi-tools.md`
    - Antigravity: `references/antigravity-tools.md`
    - Hermes Agent: `references/hermes-tools.md`
    
    ## User Instructions
    
    User instructions (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, GEMINI.md, etc, direct requests) take precedence over skills, which in turn override default behavior. Only skip skill workflows or instructions when your human partner has explicitly told you to.
